**O2 frozen contract — native Entware admission:** add `modules/entware.sh` with `cfmgr_entware_with ROOT MOUNT_PARSER STORAGE_PARSER EXPECTED_UUID EXPECTED_FS_TARGET_HEX CALLBACK [ARGS...]`, and explicit fixture `cfmgr_entware_with_test` taking the existing eight storage fixture inputs followed by expected UUID, expected filesystem-target hex, callback and optional arguments. Trusted io.sh/storage.sh are already loaded. Validate canonical nonzero lowercase UUID, canonical absolute byte-hex filesystem target (maximum 4096 decoded bytes), callback grammar and argument count before acquiring storage. Authority is independently approved caller data, never automatically learned from the current observation. Inside the retained storage callback compare UUID and filesystem-target exactly; require complete `rw` tokens and no `ro`/`noexec` tokens in both mount/superblock options. Retain existing supported ext profile checks. On admission invoke one trusted synchronous native callback with the same resolved path, complete volume ledger and caller arguments while FD8/FD9 remain held. Preserve its return status and storage cleanup/signal outcomes; output remains quiet. Return 2 for malformed API/authority, 1 for mismatched authority/unready flags, and preserve storage unsupported status 3. No opkg/package execution, filesystem write test, feature dispatch, mount operation, public CLI or authority persistence in O2. This is a checked observation/admission boundary, not an atomic write lease, proof of physical media health or permission for asynchronous users. General installed-opkg child lifetime and mount-loss handling remain the next architecture gate.

**Next O3 contract preparation — not yet launched:** native nonblocking dependency exclusion will use a stable `dependencies.lock` inode in an already trusted private RAM directory. Match the primary reference `cloudflared-common.sh` retained-file/nonblocking flock pattern, using FD7 so storage FD8/FD9 remain available. Never unlink/truncate the lock or explicitly unlock an inherited open file description. Closing the wrapper's descriptor alone must not admit a contender while a cooperative descendant still holds its copy. Matched [Merlin BusyBox flock source](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/busybox/util-linux/flock.c) implements `-n FD`; the [Linux flock interface](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/flock.2.html) defines open-file-description inheritance and release. Native command failure/absence defers; do not bootstrap locking by executing unadmitted Entware. This first ownership primitive does not prove arbitrary descendants retain FDs, general command completion, asynchronous launch/detachment or a whole-action deadline. Host kernel-lock tests and an actual Linux/BusyBox case must distinguish these boundaries before any worker integration. O2 stays the only active implementation package; freeze O3 API/ownership after O2 integration.

**O3 frozen API:** standalone source-only `modules/dependency_lock.sh`, production `cfmgr_dependency_lock_with ROOT CALLBACK [ARGS...]`, fixture `cfmgr_dependency_lock_with_test ROOT FLOCK_EXECUTABLE CALLBACK [ARGS...]`. ROOT is an already trusted private RAM directory with stable trusted ancestors; the directory and retained `dependencies.lock` must not be replaced/unlinked during any owner/descendant lifetime. Validate absolute canonical paths and callback grammar, reject root/lock symlinks and existing nonregular lock files. Use native fixed-path flock (never ambient PATH or Entware) in production; explicit fixture path only in the test API. Within an isolated shell owner open/retain FD7 in append mode with umask077, invoke exactly `flock -n 7`, and call the trusted callback with unchanged arguments only on success. Never wait/retry, unlink/truncate a lock, explicitly unlock, signal another process, run opkg or dispatch feature work. No durable/queued request or PID lock file is needed. API errors2, acquisition/tool/path failure1 (includes contention; BusyBox does not distinguish every error), uncertain command termination129; preserve callback result. Output quiet and caller flags/traps/FD7 unchanged. Normal return releases this owner's FD7; inherited copies continue exclusion until closed. This primitive requires cooperative descriptor retention and stable private directory ownership; it does not prove arbitrary package descendant completion or whole-action boundedness.

**Dependency-route correction — user instruction, 2026-10-09:** operational dependency installation must use the router's existing Entware `opkg` and its configured repositories, allowing it to select/install the required packages and resolve their libraries normally. CFMgr checks required capabilities and post-install usability; it does not become a dependency resolver or manually install dependency IPKs. Cloudflared is the explicit exception: CFMgr directly obtains/manages its release binaries for the kernel and userspace architecture/ABI combinations it can actually support. Record minimums and tested/source-supported limits; kernel architecture alone must not select a userspace binary, and an untested newer kernel/release is not automatically supported. Do not expand the bespoke direct-IPK timeout/gzip bootstrap or adopt its pinned catalogue/offline-root/`--nodeps` design as a prerequisite for ordinary dependency installation. That work is an internal, unselected proof route and historical evidence, with no operational CLI wiring or installed packages. The user subsequently authorized O1 to replace the bootstrap/tests before this pause. Complete that replacement and its gates, then stop; subsequent P1.20/P2.7 work must keep installed opkg in control. Reconcile bounded hook dispatch, missing-tool behavior, checked Entware readiness, single-worker ownership, post-install capability checks and retry handling with this route; unresolved constraints need explicit design decisions, not another inferred archive manager. This instruction supersedes earlier paragraphs calling the direct-IPK/restricted installer route selected. Preserve source findings and completed safety tests as evidence while reviewing which helpers remain useful; do not equate their coverage with completed operational dependency installation.

**Core delivery focus — parallelization without reduced quality:** optimize the time to a correctly implemented, reviewed and validated stage, alongside the test-cost objective below. At each stage, identify independent implementation, test and review work that can proceed concurrently under a shared contract and separate file ownership. Prefer this parallel route when it shortens the path to a validated result; do not maximize agent count or split tightly coupled work merely to create concurrency. Use coherent packages and concise self-contained briefs. Keep dependent integration, discrepancy resolution, validation gates and commits in the order correctness requires. Avoid redundant investigation, repeated full reviews, unnecessary handoffs and validation reruns that add no new evidence. Reuse verified findings and fix small, understood issues with the current owner when practical. When a stage slows down, identify whether investigation, implementation, handoffs, review or tests are the bottleneck and simplify that work before expanding it. **F6 frozen completion contract:** audit only materialize→fetch/archive→hash/size. Internal results are 0 completed success, 10 deliberate completed failure, 2 invalid input without cleanup authority, and 129 uncertainty. At direct synchronous native boundaries ordinary nonzero becomes10 and any signal-like result greater than128 becomes129; nested shell/helper boundaries accept only their defined results, never normalize unexpected shell1/2 into completion. Single-producer subshells explicitly exec; substitutions check status before comparing output. Public materialize/fetch/archive APIs retain0/1/2 through normalization and cannot establish cleanup. New internal `_cfmgr_bootstrap_materialize_owned TOOLS PROFILE DIRECTORY` resets `_materialize_complete=0` then sets1 only for audited internal0/10, returning0/1 respectively; all other results retain completion0 and return129. Preserve the old closure-size consumer interface with a shared audited size body/entry; later closure build/hash/image errors remain F5-retained. Reset caught EXIT/HUP/INT/TERM traps only within audited subshell supervisors, preserving isolation-owner traps and the existing trust in synchronous native tools that leave no background descendants.

Isolation marks staging attempted before acquisition so completed failure still requires fresh private RAM/root identity before cleanup. Complete acquisition failure may clear prepare and return1; uncertainty retains it. Successful acquisition keeps prepare through all later image/root preparation. No disk terminal record, new polling loop, asynchronous launch, process search, signal sending, stale-guard recovery or aggregate-deadline claim. This is a narrow retry foundation, not final R35/R36 acceptance. [POSIX shell command status](https://pubs.opengroup.org/onlinepubs/9799919799/utilities/V3_chap02.html) specifies signal termination above128; its possible larger values must be normalized before internal return. Saved native ash getstatus source additionally uses128+signal, but current CI and eventual Merlin acceptance remain distinct.

The fixture required EROFS when opening its own running timeout executable for write. [Linux file_get_write_access](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/fs/open.c#L828-L835) checks inode write access (ETXTBSY for active executable text) before mount write access, so this assertion conflated two rejection mechanisms. Use the separate unexecuted gzip copy for the existing-executable EROFS check; preserve strict EROFS for that file, new-file creation and libc. Parent/Sol agree on this source-supported correction; the first captured actor stderr was removed during normal cleanup, so that log alone does not prove the exact errno. ## Requirements

| ID | Required outcome | Completion evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| R01 | One installable CFMgr entry point with focused nested menus and dedicated DDNS, Cloudflared, and IP-Sync setup flows. | Base install creates the manager/default config with all three unconfigured and disabled. Each main action opens its own submenu; only the chosen feature's setup installs/configures that feature. |
| R02 | Port and improve the supplied setup, while allowing cleaner internal organization. | Each inventory/audit row has a mapped equivalent and observable parity check; record deliberate changes instead of silently dropping behavior. Remove all three reference Python call paths, preserve the explicit Access read-only dry run, and retain lifecycle/queue safeguards. Source review alone never completes runtime parity. |
| R03 | Configuration-driven DDNS, tunnel, Access IP publishing, logging, and backup settings. | No account-specific values in code; versioned configuration can be exported and restored. |
| R04 | Prompt only for credentials/settings needed by selected features and keep router runtime dependencies minimal. | POSIX shell with documented native/Entware prerequisites under R36/D55; cloudflared added only for tunnel scope and optional logging remains separate. No Python/venv/picker router dependency. Python/pytest requirements are development-only under R26. |
| R05 | One tunnel/router through A token or B advanced browser-login/create/import. | A saves supplied runtime token. B Auto-detect/import? [y/N]: y validates supplied YAML/JSON/optional used cert; n/Enter reviewed Save creates selected named tunnel/starter, reusing validated same-account cert or supported login if absent. Show terminal-only browser URL/wait/current-attempt validation. Explicit fresh replacement preserves old generation/foreign files, never deletes old remote tunnel; ongoing YAML ownership R32. |
| R06 | Preserve cloudflared automatic binary updates, watchdog recovery, readiness checks, bounded stop, and interrupted-update rollback. | A/B and explicit maintenance install use the shared checked official-release downloader on mounted Entware, with no feed-package requirement. Saved On authorizes watchdog recovery after an external kill; CFMgr Stop means Off, Start means On, and Restart never enables Off. |
| R07 | DDNS reconciles current router IPv4 A and IPv6 AAAA state with truthful Merlin completion. | Primary IPv4-only/dual-stack paths update active families, delete confirmed inactive managed records and recreate them when active again. Fresh remote comparison before no-op; local authority overwrites remote drift within configured scope. IPv6-only stays provisional R34; unknown source is not deletion evidence. |
| R08 | Optional router-authoritative IPv4/IPv6 Zero Trust list publishing. | One shared or two guided/mapped targets equal current active host sets; remove inactive/extra addresses and clear empty targets without deleting list IDs. Fresh read/compare/no-op, automatic drift repair and current-state retries; no policy edits. Pure shell; IPv6 evidence qualified R34. |
| R09 | Integrate with boot, WAN, DDNS, and mount hooks without damaging other add-ons. | Separate identifiable CFMgr feature/maintenance blocks; exact owned edits, whole-hook syntax checks and concurrent-change detection; unrelated content/order/control flow preserved. Enabled DDNS/IP-Sync alone have operational blocks/jobs, with exactly one owned cron entry per task. |
| R10 | Verify Entware before required package or Cloudflared/custom-logging work. | Shared jq/timeout/SHA256 prerequisites make mounted usable Entware a normal operational requirement under R36/D55. Native entry/recovery diagnostics remain available when it fails. Persist settings/recovery in JFFS and queues in RAM; missing logging alone does not block publishing. Verify installed/mounted/writable Entware before any `/opt` mutation; never write into a stale mountpoint. |
| R11 | Keep authoritative credential records and a bounded original account-certificate recovery copy in private JFFS, native state RAM, and live tunnel files/logs on Entware. | Main private config holds full runtime values/refs/expected identity; `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/cert.pem` holds validated original account-certificate bytes. User-owned YAML/derived JSON/live certificate/binary/logs remain on verified Entware. Prove practical certificate JFFS/space bounds and native parsers. Provider settings and RAM queues stay outside /opt; operational tools follow R36/D55. Ordinary manual backup includes both owned certificate copies and full user YAML from Entware. |
| R12 | Give configured features separate Scribe-managed logs with independent rotation and explicit scoped logging removal. | First fresh ready setup applies feature policy; Cloudflared tuning editable, native-feature policies fixed. Service Stop/Off preserves logging preference/logs/rules and Scribe continues rotation independently. Per-feature Uninstall logging disables owned output/self-heal and deletes found owned logs/rotations even if Scribe was removed; it never controls Scribe daemon/job/global policy. Native messages remain. |
| R13 | Provide bounded scoped Scribe checks/repair and explicit Install/enable or Uninstall logging after setup. | Re-detect is per-feature; saved opt-out never silently enables logging. Automatic repair requires requested logging and usable Scribe; uninstall needs no Scribe re-detect/heal dependency. Guard/save inactive preference before quiescing writer/deletion; remove identifiable owned rule/include only when Scribe detected and storage safe, never rebuild missing base. Missing/read-only storage reports deferred cleanup without stale writes/false success. |
| R14 | Let the user select a mounted backup drive and create manual `.tar.gz` backups with a simple rolling limit of 3–5 archives. | Root-level `CFBackup`, numbered archive selection, router-local date/time labels, and oldest-first pruning only after a new archive verifies. No backup cron/automatic archive creation. Include eligible owned Cloudflared/DDNS/IP-Sync logs/history; internal recovery copies remain separate. |
| R15 | Support data-only CFMgr backup/restore with separate feature setup/activation and Cloudflared maintenance states. | Archive coherent config/state/tunnel data/drafts/log preferences/eligible history plus owned JFFS certificate recovery copy, never executable code/binaries/hooks/jobs/generated rules or live transaction journals. Current trusted CFMgr validates/migrates supported data schemas and rebuilds setup with current installers/templates; unsupported/newer schemas fail before writes. Off stays unhooked; activate eligible saved On/maintenance only after fresh validation/generation/readiness. |
| R16 | Protect credentials in config/invocation/diagnostics and make backup privacy clear. | Private runtime config, safe parsing/redaction and repository secret checks. Native unencrypted backups include credentials; existing review/docs say user is responsible for archive privacy. Best-effort private backup modes, no encryption dependency or drive rejection solely for lacking POSIX modes; safe paths/mount/writability checks remain. |
| R17 | Support repair and clean removal of CFMgr-owned integrations. | Full uninstall KEEP detaches owned hooks/services/cron, removes Cloudflared/watchdog/updater package components and owned log integration/files, but retains master authority, primary Entware config.yml/JSON/cert, JFFS cert copy, activation/preferences, retained marker and inventoried CFMgr-owned config.yml.bak. WIPE/reset remove all owned tunnel data/cert copy/.bak after safe cleanup. Entware/Scribe globals/foreign files/integrations/manual archives/remote resources survive; missing or invalid required storage defers cleanup honestly. |
| R18 | Keep this plan usable as a handoff during development. | Phase/checklist status, decisions, commits, validation evidence, and next actions are updated after each material package. |
| R19 | Offer `0) Check status` at the top of the main menu with configured/enabled-aware output. | Show basic manager information; disabled features print only `Disabled` and `Configured: true/false`. Enabled features show relevant runtime/sync/logging details. No configured feature means prompt to configure at least one; status never installs, enables, syncs, repairs, or changes services. |
| R20 | Verified manual manager update/forced reinstall through `u`/`uf`, with mixed menu keys and consistent `e` navigation. | Trusted release manifest binds SHA256/file inventory/version/schema to the selected release/commit; read embedded script version as data before execution. `u` skips current; `uf` may reinstall current but never downgrade/reset/bypass validation. Before mutation, boundedly compare safely parsed master authority, the owned YAML trio and the full derived JSON. Healthy matching tunnel state is a true runtime no-op: no forced restore/overwrite, JFFS resnapshot, tunnel restart/binary download, provider call, or activation change. The selected manager package may still be transferred when the user confirms a newer release or `uf`. Repair only validated owned drift/missing files under locks/guards/generation; preserve user-owned YAML/ingress differences and restart once only for real runtime change; Off stays Off. `developer=false` defaults; manual-file-only true suppresses checks/offers/external callbacks, while explicitly confirmed `uf` may replace local code with full validation. No startup check or manager cron. |

Independent `maintenance_enabled` authorizes updater only with saved auto-update preference; watchdog/start also require configured+enabled. Off clears both activation flags and removes all owned tunnel/maintenance hooks/jobs but preserves data/binary/preferences. Maintenance uninstall also removes verified owned binary/helpers; later configured On reinstalls them. |
| R22 | B fresh/replacement CLI-create or advanced import. | n/blank reviewed Save reuses validated same-account cert or guarded browser login, then selected named create/private JSON/starter. y imports advanced files without login/create. Ordinary launch/boot/watchdog/missing cert alone never logs in; valid UUID+JSON runtime unaffected. Explicit existing-UUID credential repair may login/fetch under R33/D44; failed NEW creation follows manual dashboard cleanup D43. A token-only. |
| R23 | Persist resumable native setup drafts and recover interrupted Cloudflared commits safely. | DDNS/IP-Sync checkpoint completed token/account/zone/selection inputs privately before final Save; e exits setup, Ctrl+C exits safely, next Setup resumes missing/invalid fields. Draft never activates or overwrites working config. Cloudflared pre-Save may cancel; reviewed Save/config mutation disables e until terminal state, Ctrl+C leaves guarded incomplete state when completion/rollback unproved. Next setup/launch enters recovery first; preserve credentials/foreign work and never blindly recreate a tunnel. |
| R24 | Self-heal owned hooks/jobs according to saved activation state without resetting configuration. | DDNS/IP-Sync Off removes their operational blocks/jobs; Cloudflared Off removes all owned runtime and maintenance blocks/jobs. On restores eligible definitions; healthy launch repair follows active flags and never downloads/enables. Preserve saved credentials/options and foreign content; ambiguous damage requires repair. |
| R25 | Nonblocking Entware gates at boot and throughout Cloudflared work, preserving readiness/outage recovery. | Register saved eligible jobs through JFFS even before mount. Each handler checks its own prerequisites; expected mount loss interrupts owned setup/install/login/create/repair work boundedly, guards partial data and reports unavailable storage without stale writes or waiting. Matching mount/tick resumes only previously configured eligible work, never failed creation. Native recovery/status/Off/unhook remain usable; dependent DDNS/IP-Sync work waits under R36/D55; NTP/ping/DNS capped retry/full reset retained. |

Router runtime remains POSIX shell with R36/D55 prerequisites. Build verified setup/operation guides and sanitized Cloudflare screenshots in `docs/`/`.images/` as features become available; main README links them. |
| R27 | Share one firmware-native address resolver/dispatcher between DDNS and IP-Sync, with separate IPv4/IPv6 observations and no duplicated discovery code. | Preserve automatic IPv4 NAT/CGNAT detection, add selected-WAN global IPv6 host validation, family-specific source/egress/freshness and independent success. Prefer eligible DDNS observations; standalone IP-Sync resolves either/both without DNS setup. One RAM cache/lock/schedule with per-family records, shared R36/D55 prerequisites and no router Python dependency. |
| R28 | Preserve exact saved setup and activation intent across retained-config full uninstall and first reinstallation. | Detect retained identity/config/data before defaults; rebuild only validated configured/prepared maintenance setup with current code, no wizard or remote recreation. Preserve saved enabled intent under installation guard during removal; Off stays Off/unhooked, missing Entware defers dependent reconstruction while native recovery/status remains available. Invalid retained data enters repair, never blank defaults. |
| R29 | Provide explicitly confirmed passive reset with interruption-safe cleanup. | `ao` Initialise / reset everything reuses full-wipe cleanup but retains verified manager package; fresh defaults all false/off and developer=false only after complete owned cleanup. Durable intent suppresses old enabled work before stops/deletion. Cancel/empty input cannot wipe; missing/read-only mount defers/refuses completion; foreign state/backups/remote resources survive. |
| R30 | Avoid redundant native writes while automatically reconciling remote drift and current family removal. | Every eligible publishing run reads configured targets; no write only when complete remote state matches current router/config desired state. Last-success cache alone never skips the read. Correct changed values/extra entries and remove confirmed inactive families within managed scope. Cooldowns/transient failures keep On with newest current-state retry work. |
| R31 | Contain authoritative credential/permission failures through public Off and minimal deduplicated repair. | Persist non-secret fault/credential reference/prior-requested On before Off/unhooking. Only source-tested definitive auth evidence triggers this, not generic errors/PID/connection count/throttling. Initial failure opens failed-credential-only entry; launch asks Fix invalid credentials now? [Y/n], conditional fx reopens it. Cancel stays Off; repair only faulted/requested On work, preserve intentional Off and other settings. |
| R32 | Scoped runtime authority and user-owned B application YAML with a JFFS account-certificate recovery copy. | Main full AccountTag/TunnelID/TunnelSecret/expected refs derives SAME JSON; reconcile ONLY tunnel/credentials-file/selected origincert, preserving other YAML/comments/ingress. File reconstruction is not auth success; rejected saved credentials use R33. Store validated original account-cert bytes at `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/cert.pem` with account/format/provenance/hash/generation metadata. Do not duplicate user-owned YAML in JFFS: KEEP preserves the complete primary Entware YAML. Backup/restore includes the JFFS cert copy and full runtime YAML coherently. KEEP retains both Entware/JFFS certificate copies and owned .bak; WIPE/reset remove the owned JFFS cert copy and Entware files. Reviewed replacement preserves user YAML/private owned config.yml.bak under D44. |
| R33 | Recover existing credentials first; offer explicit replacement only when recovery cannot succeed. | Missing JSON derives from validated main authority once per generation. Rejected A token: request existing tunnel dashboard connector token. Rejected B runtime: explicit same-account login if needed, then supported existing-UUID credential fetch to fresh private JSON, validate/store/retry without changing UUID. Login alone does not recover secret. Unsupported/deleted/unrecoverable runtime offers reviewed recreation/manual old-tunnel dashboard deletion, preserves ingress/full application YAML and private .bak. Throttle/network/drive loss/repairable permission never imply recreation; e/decline keeps affected Off. |
| R34 | Provisional IPv6 with honest evidence and authoritative active-family cleanup. | Primary IPv4-only/dual-stack scenarios include IPv6 disable/removal/return. Keep symmetric IPv6-only support provisional without a required firmware mode. Planned until implemented, then untested/partially tested with actual coverage; full live IPv6 testing optional, safety fixtures required. Router /128 still does not cover other LAN hosts. |
| R35 | No CFMgr feature may hold up router boot or cause reboot/restart loops; start work only when its prerequisites are ready. | Boot uses the existing outage/readiness handling: missing required mount, unsynced NTP or unusable Internet leaves affected startup/publishing/update work waiting until all scoped gates pass. DDNS, IP-Sync, Cloudflared, discovery, watchdog, updater, logging and recovery obey bounded dispatch, detached I/O, admission before spawn and capped retries. No automatic router reboot/global service restart or recursive hook/recovery loop. Tunnel drops and Internet return use the same controller. Native recovery remains available when shared Entware prerequisites fail; firmware/runtime evidence Pending P1.19/V73. |
| R36 | Minimal verified Entware prerequisites, retryable failed installation and an independent dependency reinstall action. | Check/install required packages on launch and manager install/update/reinstall. Failure or unusable post-install tools block operational work; next launch checks again and can retry. Advanced options provides Reinstall Entware dependencies for CFMgr's selected packages only, preserving settings/activation and distinct from Cloudflared daemon/hooks/workers reinstall. Native OpenSSL remains bootstrap/edge fallback; SHA256 package is required. Document kernel/ABI/feed boundaries and direct/transitive packages. P1.20/P2.7/V74. |
| R37 | A safe standalone health check with --diagnostic and --doctor aliases. | Both arguments run the same report without normal startup or dependency repair. Show available router model/firmware/kernel/userspace/tool context and stable PASS/FAIL/SKIP check IDs. Test actual runtime commands/options with synthetic bounded work; distinguish missing packages, unavailable Entware, broken tools, unsupported arguments and dependent skips. No config/service/provider/package mutations; private temporary probe files only. Redact secrets and sensitive identifiers. P2.8/V75. |

All three fully configured features also expose quick `cfmgr start <service>` / `cfmgr stop <service>` shortcuts through the same On/Off handlers, with strict configured/integrity gates, normalized case/aliases and truthful per-step notices as defined below. These are planned interfaces only.

## Current setup inventory

All paths in this table are relative to the supplied `cloudflared/setup/` reference. Real domains, IPs, IDs, tokens, credential contents, and certificate contents are intentionally omitted.

| Source | Current responsibility and evidence | CFMgr equivalent |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `jffs/scripts/services-start` | Lines 5–8 register watchdog, updater, daily DDNS, and Access retry jobs. Other lines start existing add-ons. | Managed schedule registration through a small owned hook block. |
| `jffs/scripts/wan-event` | Lines 3–9 start the tunnel in a detached worker after a 20-second settle delay for WAN unit `0` becoming `connected`; scMerlin still receives the event. | WAN handler using the shared lifecycle controller; preserve event forwarding. |
| `jffs/scripts/ddns-start` | Lines 30–40 invoke Python to enumerate DNS record IDs; 63–138 resolve IPv4, launch the Python Access helper at 79–80, update A/optional AAAA and report completion. No `/opt` path does not mean no Python dependency. Settings remain private. | Replace both Python record parsing and Access notification with native equivalents. Preserve all-record updates and independent DNS/list outcomes; strengthen structured API validation. |
| `jffs/scripts/cloudflared.sh` | Shared-helper entry point; default/start and restart commands take the lifecycle lock. Lines 40–52 check readiness before a restart stops the service. | `cfmgr start`, `restart`, and watchdog entry points. |
| `jffs/scripts/cloudflared-common.sh` | Paths at lines 9–16; readiness at 71–200, including `ntp_ready=1`, two bounded IPv4 ping targets and tunnel DNS; launch at 203–229; pending recovery at 233–397; bounded stop at 399–453. Snapshot checks files/executables but has no mount-identity gate or growing outage retry timer. | Shared lifecycle preserves readiness tests; add verified Entware gating and capped outage backoff/full recovery reset. |
| `jffs/scripts/cloudflared-update.sh` | Official GitHub release lookup, fixed Linux ARM64 asset, version validation, staging, separate update lock, `.old` backup, pending marker, activation, and rollback. | Architecture-aware updater retaining these protections. |
| `jffs/scripts/cloudflare-access-ip.py` | Python 2.7/3 stdlib plus curl; private adjacent JSON, public-IP/list/readback validation, flock newest-request queue/retries under `/tmp/cloudflare-access-ip`; no `/opt` paths. | POSIX-shell IP-Sync with JFFS settings/RAM queue and R36/D55 prerequisites. Python JSON/fcntl semantics require a validated native replacement, not an unchanged helper or grep-only parser. |
| `jffs/scripts/cloudflare-access-ip.json` | Enabled flag and account/list identity plus token settings. This supplied file is enabled; values are private. | Import into private external configuration; new installs keep unconfigured features disabled. |
| `opt/etc/cloudflared/config.yml` | Tunnel reference, credential-file path, origin-certificate path, and one `http_status` ingress entry. | Import the complete configuration without inventing application routes. |
| `opt/etc/cloudflared/<tunnel-id>.json` | Tunnel credentials with `AccountTag`, `TunnelSecret`, and `TunnelID` fields. | Private tunnel credentials on Entware storage. |
| `opt/etc/cloudflared/cert.pem` | Existing account certificate referenced by the YAML configuration. | Preserve on import; require it only for workflows that actually use certificate-based administration. |
| The two script READMEs | Existing behavior, deployment grouping, permissions, schedules, and operator checks. | Use as parity evidence; move public operating documentation into `docs/` as implemented. |

The file layout alone does not establish the remote tunnel's `config_src` or its actual published applications. Re-detect/import must validate the intended runtime method and preserve existing remote/local routing rather than replacing it with guessed wizard output. The reference YAML is import evidence, not a reason to make advanced mode the default for fresh installations.

## Porting audit and remaining edge cases

Review completed against the supplied source on 2026-10-08 from CFMgr base `5ceb412`; all six published fingerprints above still match. This is a source/specification audit, with no reference-script execution or live-router/provider test. The plan covers the main capabilities and intentionally extends them; the following mapping makes the porting obligations and remaining gaps explicit. Earlier inventory wording missed Python inside DDNS record enumeration and is corrected above.

**Runtime language boundary:** IP-Sync is a complete rewrite as BusyBox-compatible POSIX `sh`, using verified firmware tools, bundled shell/awk helpers and the selected Entware prerequisites in R36/D55. Preserve the reference's observable behavior and safeguards, not its Python implementation. Do not ship, invoke, wrap or fall back to `cloudflare-access-ip.py`, Python or a virtualenv on the router. DDNS record parsing and updater metadata parsing use the same verified shell/JSON facilities. Python/pytest is confined to developer/CI tests and excluded from the runtime package.

### Baseline behavior to retain and deliberate improvements

Source paths/lines below are relative to `cloudflared/setup/jffs/scripts/`. Private source settings are neither quoted nor copied into fixtures.

| Baseline evidence | Porting obligation / deliberate improvement | Planned verification |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ddns-start:30–40,63–138` | Replace Python parsing/Access dispatch and updater metadata Python. Preserve configured matching A/AAAA updates, imported TTL/proxy and one firmware result; strengthen typed complete API validation. D53 deliberately adds inactive-family deletion and configured-record recreation on return, superseding the update-only rule. | P4.1–P4.3; V07/V42/V58/V61/V72 |
| `ddns-start:63–80` | Share validated per-family observations with detached IP-Sync before DNS work; DNS failure cannot suppress a valid list notification, and list failure cannot delay or fail DDNS completion. The selected NAT-aware resolver intentionally strengthens raw legacy WAN input. | P4.1–P4.3; V07/V44/V50 |
| `cloudflare-access-ip.py:134–178` | Preserve account/target identity, private API transport, complete readback and unchanged no-write. Replace old max-one-IPv4/content-refusal guards with D53 authoritative active-family sets, removal of extra/inactive values, empty-list clearing and remote-drift overwrite inside configured targets. Preserve unmanaged metadata; no guessed target/policy. | P4.2/P4.4; V08/V58/V67/V72 |
| `cloudflare-access-ip.py:206–221` | Retain an explicit provider-read-only target-check equivalent of legacy `--dry-run IPv4`, extended to explicit IPv4 and/or IPv6 candidates, including for configured-but-Off IP-Sync. Validate saved config/public candidate and read the target; verified results report unchanged/would-replace without PUT, discovery, queue/success-cache/history writes or enabling. Deliberate strengthening: shared cooldown and definitive saved-credential auth-Off containment still apply; failures never report a successful check. This is explicit, never ordinary status/launch. | P4.2/P5.3; V58 |
| `cloudflare-access-ip.py:98–117,182–272` | Preserve private curl auth, short queue/nonblocking worker locks, child-held writer exclusion, newest work and four-attempt bound. Reference uses saved-IP-only retry and 24-hour expiry/negative-age rejection; D53 deliberately revalidates current family/config state and uses the shared resolver when needed, preventing stale add/remove replay. Freeze monotonic/budget bounds. | P1.5/P1.11/P4.2; V08/V42/V59/V66 |
| `cloudflared-common.sh:71–229,343–453` | NTP, either of two bounded IPv4 pings, SRV/A checks, readiness before normal restart, two-second startup observation and 35-second normal stop bound. Add mount verification, owned-process identity and capped retries without turning process survival into connectivity proof. | P3.1; V03–V04/V18/V41/V64 |
| `cloudflared-update.sh:132–148,175–332`; `cloudflared-common.sh:233–397` | Preserve same-version skip, downgrade refusal, locked rechecks, verified old binary, pending recovery and failed-update reporting even after successful rollback. Add native metadata/digest/ABI checks and separate idle maintenance recovery; never transplant the reference's unconditional runtime start into maintenance-only work. | P3.2; V05–V06/V36/V45/V60 |
| `cloudflared-common.sh:214–215`; `services-start:2–15`; `wan-event:3–9` | Detached daemon closes controller/update lock descriptors; one shared controller, four existing schedule defaults, detached 20-second WAN settle and immediate original-argument forwarding remain. Delayed work rechecks current state; stronger foreign-process/hook ownership is intentional. | P3.1/P5.1; V03/V09/V40/V59/V64 |

### Gaps resolved in the specification and proof still required

These are acceptance contracts, not claims of implemented fixes. They refine existing requirements; no new router dependency, provider administration feature or deployment is authorized.

| Gap / edge case | Required resolution / remaining decision | Owner / evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Lock lifetime differs by child role | Keep stable lock-file inodes; never unlink a live lock to unlock it. A long-lived connector must close lifecycle/update/config/queue descriptors before detach, while an in-flight provider child must retain its writer exclusion until it exits or is safely reaped. Define a descriptor/lock-order map, bounded child cleanup and boot/PID-start identity checks, including PID reuse and foreign same-name processes. | P1.5/P2/P3/P4; V59 |
| Idle binary recovery conflicts with startup-only clearance | Split artifact transaction completion from runtime activation evidence. Off/unconfigured maintenance validates installed artifact/digest/ABI/version and durable transaction identity without credentials, tunnel DNS or launching a connector. Interrupted idle replacement resolves files/backup first; no permanent startup-required marker. For On activation, preserve readiness recheck, fresh owned-child evidence, protected backup and honest rollback result. A concurrent Off wins. | P1.8/P3.2; V60 |
| Native transport can inherit unsafe settings or exceed RAM bounds | Use native curl with `-q` first; isolate unapproved proxy/CA/config environment, shell tracing and token output. Authenticated API requests do not follow unapproved redirects; release redirects use a separate bounded HTTPS policy without provider credentials. Enforce streamed body/header and total-operation limits even without Content-Length. Verify installed curl behavior; `--max-filesize` alone is insufficient on older curl for unknown-size responses. | P1.11/P4; V42/V61; [curl manual](https://curl.se/docs/manpage.html) |
| DDNS completion, enumeration and IPv6 need concrete cases | Freeze owned callback identity/overlap/cooldown and aggregate budget. Complete authenticated empty lookup confirms inactive-family absence or permits recreation of configured active definitions; failed/truncated lookup does neither. Confirmed disable/absence deletes managed A/AAAA; return recreates them. Unknown source/type conflicts/partial outcomes never count as full success or suppress valid IP-Sync source state. | P1.14/P1.16/P4.1; V07/V61/V72 |
| Confirmed cache can hide remote drift indefinitely | D53 requires current complete target reads before no-op in every eligible publishing run, even unchanged local IP. Existing coalesced cron/events correct drift automatically; cache is past evidence only. No separate auth poll or menu/status API work. Verify list clear and managed DNS removal/recreation, lost responses and actual cadence. | D48/D53/P1.14/P4.3; V50/V62/V72 |
| Shared config and multi-file manager publication | At accepted wizard Save, read latest valid config under short lock and overwrite only explicitly accepted fields, including reviewed activation if present. Manual edits to those fields do not force conflict review. Preserve unrelated/guard/auth state; an Off after Save wins. Detect raw content changes without revision bumps and retry bounded scoped merge. Keep compatible package generation per invocation and no lock across input/login. | D53/P1.3/P1.18/P2.5/P3.3; V63 |
| Failure to persist a guard | Pre-change guard/config/space/rename failure aborts the requested mutation, retaining old valid files. If an observed auth fault cannot be durably saved, contain verified owned work with a boot-local guard and report persistence failure; do not claim durable Off or reboot safety. Freeze writable/recovery guarantees and safe behavior if RAM/JFFS are full/read-only before implementing state transitions. | P1.3/P2.5–P2.6; V63 |
| Per-request deadlines do not bound the whole action | Freeze aggregate budgets for discovery fallback, DNS pagination/records, queue batches, repeated readiness/version probes and hook shutdown. Coalesce bursts even across successive WAN/mount invocations; events cannot repeatedly bypass backoff/server delay. Recheck On/generation after the WAN settle delay. Establish temporary unmount/shutdown suppression before stopping so a watchdog cannot respawn against a still-mounted departing volume; preserve saved On for later eligible recovery. | P1.10/P1.13–P1.16/P3/P4/P5; V41/V64 |
| Loss of terminal/input | EOF or absent TTY is not a default-yes credential repair answer, destructive confirmation, or infinite prompt loop. HUP/INT/TERM use the existing pre-/post-Save cleanup contract; restore terminal echo after masked input. Noninteractive hooks fail/defer without prompting. Reject or escape control characters in names/notices and treat credential/config strings as data. | P2.2/P2.5/P5.3; V26/V38/V64 |
| Archive/input validation and full YAML dependencies | Parse hostile manifest/archive input with limits on compressed/expanded bytes, entries, paths, depth and metadata; verify the exact bytes later restored, not a swappable pathname. Reject unsupported link/device/extended-header/path tricks before extraction can escape staging. Inventory user YAML's additional referenced files separately: include only explicitly owned allowlisted data, preserve foreign dependencies and report required missing external files/remapping before activation. Never claim a portable complete backup merely because YAML+JSON+cert were saved. | P1.4/P1.11/P7; V16/V54/V65 |

The [Merlin DDNS contract](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/wiki/Custom-DDNS) makes the custom hook responsible for completion and notes firmware retry behavior on failures. That makes cooldown/overlap handling a required firmware contract test, not just a Cloudflare API test. [DNS editing](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/dns/subresources/records/methods/edit/) and [list replacement](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/zero_trust/subresources/gateway/subresources/lists/methods/update/) also require resource-specific validation; do not generalize one endpoint's response, pagination or update semantics to another.

P1 exits only when the supported native parser/YAML subset, clocks, lock descriptors, credential evidence, installed binary capabilities, memory/space/budget limits and unresolved callback/cache policies are recorded with fixtures or explicitly scoped support limits. Do not start a dependent implementation package with an unresolved behavioral contradiction. New features already selected elsewhere remain selected; this audit does not add multi-tunnel management, automatic whole-list/tunnel deletion, extra daemons or new package dependencies. D53 permits managed address/record reconciliation within its configured scope.

## Hooks and schedules

The existing integration has several entry points, but they must share one controller. Merlin documents the boot, WAN, DDNS, mount, and unmount hooks in its [user-script reference](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/wiki/User-scripts).

CFMgr-owned blocks in `services-start`, `wan-event`, `ddns-start`, DHCP/mount/stop hooks and owned cron commands must be thin dispatchers into `/jffs/scripts/cfmgr` in explicit noninteractive handler mode, or a fixed CFMgr-owned shell handler. That entry loads the same global common POSIX shell file, proposed `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/common.sh`, for activation/config guards, scoped readiness/outage waiting, admission/locks, deadlines and retry scheduling. DDNS and IP-Sync both use it whenever configured and On, as does Cloudflared; feature handlers retain only their provider-specific operations and share the native address resolver. No copied readiness loops, API calls, credentials, source selection, lifecycle logic or WAN settle sleeps inside hooks.

Forward original hook arguments safely and unchanged to the selected handler and preserve foreign add-on arguments/control flow. The handler validates its event/arguments, admits or coalesces bounded work, and returns within the hook budget; direct handler/cron/manual entry cannot bypass the same gates. Do not source the common library into a foreign hook's shell or run the interactive menu from a hook. Loading the common file defines helpers/constants only; it must not start services, wait, perform network work or mutate router state. Missing/incompatible manager/common files leave affected CFMgr work inactive with a bounded notice, while boot and other hook content continue. Package the entry/helpers/common file as one compatible verified generation; exact dispatch arguments/private helper split are P1/P2 work.

| Hook/trigger | Current setup | Planned integration and preservation rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `services-start` | Registers the four Cloudflare jobs; also invokes YazDHCP, scMerlin/tailtaintdns, amtm history, BackupMon, and MerlinAU. | Separate JFFS blocks register saved eligible jobs independently of Entware and request detached work. Native DDNS/IP-Sync follow their own gates; Cloudflared checks mount and defers if absent. Off native blocks are absent; Cloudflared runtime/maintenance blocks exist only under their respective activation flags. Preserve foreign control flow; no blocking wait, API or download in this hook. |
| `wan-event` | Unit `0`/`connected`, detached 20-second delay, then shared start; scMerlin receives the original arguments. | Preserve this tunnel baseline/default with configurable WAN selection/settle policy. Also request one coalesced detached shared IPv4/IPv6 discovery for enabled native features on the selected WAN's connected/reconnected event. Pass original arguments intact to other add-ons; no network work/wait in the hook. |
| `dhcpc-event` (conditional native integration) | No IPv6 discovery integration claimed in reference snapshot. | Where supported, use documented IPv6 event/family arguments to coalesce detached selected-WAN address/prefix/RA discovery (bound/updated/rebound/ra-updated). Preserve foreign content/arguments; no network wait. P1 proves firmware event/interface mapping; shared cron covers missed/unsupported events. |
| `ddns-start` | Reference owns DNS/completion and forwards IPv4 to Python Access. | Exactly one completion owner. Validate IPv4 `$1` and optional IPv6 `$2` independently, forward valid observations to shell IP-Sync. Apply R35 bounded dispatch/completion, detached publishing and reentry protection; P1.19 must prove the firmware callback contract, never assume asynchronous completion works. IP-Sync cannot delay DDNS completion. Migrate recognized legacy provider code; unknown custom content needs reviewed integration, never duplicate updaters. |
| Access-only DDNS observation | Not separately configured in the reference. | Only while configured IP-Sync is On and CFMgr DNS is disabled, preserve the existing provider and add the owned IP-Sync observation block; route its candidate through the common resolver and do not report another provider's DDNS result. Standalone shared boot/WAN/discovery triggers also work when no DDNS provider exists. IP-Sync Off removes its observation block without touching the provider. |
| `post-mount` | Outside the primary reference: captured amtm/Entware mounting and other add-ons, including Scribe rotation registration. | Detached recheck only for actual Entware mount: resume eligible deferred Cloudflared/maintenance and eligible logging integration under health/enable/preference gates. DDNS/IP-Sync already operate independently; do not force sync or activate every service. Ignore unrelated mounts and preserve helpers/Scribe schedule. |
| `unmount` / `services-stop` | No CFMgr entries in the supplied reference. | Set suppression before bounded owned-worker/tunnel stop; match the actual Entware mount and preserve saved On intent. The stop deadline must fit the verified firmware hook budget, even when shorter than the normal 35-second ceiling. No respawn during teardown, indefinite wait or reboot recovery. |
| Watchdog/manual/update/restore | Existing watchdog, manual restart, and updater converge on shared lifecycle helpers. | All future service transitions use the same locks/readiness/stop/start implementation. |

Use stable comment identifiers such as `# CFMgr DDNS BEGIN` / `# CFMgr DDNS END`, with separate `IP-Sync`, `Cloudflared`, `Maintenance` and shared `NativeIP` blocks plus a private ownership manifest. `NativeIP` replaces the earlier plan-only `NativeIPv4` label; no deployed CFMgr marker migration is claimed. Retain the family-neutral `CFMgrPublicIP` job name; never rewrite foreign markers. The shared native block/job remains only while at least one valid DDNS/IP-Sync feature is On. This allows one feature to be removed without deleting another feature's calls. For example, the markers in a shared startup hook can look like this (illustrative; dispatch code is not implemented yet):

```sh
# CFMgr DDNS BEGIN
# CFMgr-owned DDNS startup/scheduler dispatch goes here.
# CFMgr DDNS END
```

Re-running install/repair must not duplicate blocks. Remove/replace only the exact identified CFMgr-owned block; never replace a whole populated hook, strip all matching words, or delete another add-on's markers. Preserve unrelated content, shebang, argument forwarding, existing branch/exit/return behavior, executable mode, and POSIX shell syntax. Choose a reachable insertion point instead of appending after an existing exit. Stage edits, syntax-check the complete hook, compare its current content again under the appropriate lock, and atomically replace only when the pre-edit snapshot still matches; changed content requires a fresh merge/retry, never a lost concurrent update. A missing hook may be created as a valid minimal script. A truncated/mismatched marker is repairable only when trusted ownership/snapshot evidence identifies the range safely; ambiguous foreign damage stays untouched and is reported.

Fresh base installation adds no feature blocks/jobs; first installation after retained-config removal uses the reconstruction contract below. Dedicated setup/import manages only selected eligible integration. DDNS/IP-Sync Off have no operational blocks/jobs; Cloudflared runtime requires configured+enabled and maintenance requires maintenance_enabled. Explicit maintenance install can activate updater maintenance while tunnel stays Off/unconfigured; a later explicit Off clears both activation flags. Merely opening menus/status never installs, downloads, enables or changes remote resources.

Boot/WAN/DDNS/mount/watchdog/cron handlers never run a CFMgr package update check, prompt for an update, or reinstall the manager. Manager package updates are explicit actions; cloudflared's existing scheduled binary update remains a separate configured job.

| Current job | Current schedule (router local time) | Proposed CFMgr job | Activation rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Cloudflared` | `* * * * *` | `CFMgrWatchdog` | Trusted installed/configured/enabled generation with no affecting recovery/removal guard; register even before mount, defer each invocation until readiness. External process kill leaves On and permits recovery. No watchdog for Off/unconfigured/maintenance-only setup. |
| `CloudflaredUpdate` | `37 3 * * *` | `CFMgrCloudflaredUpdate` | Installed + maintenance_enabled + saved auto-update preference, with no affecting guard; independent of tunnel enable/configured state. Each run checks mount before binary/network work. Preference alone creates no job; idle update starts no tunnel. |
| `UpdateDDNS` | `0 0 * * *` | `CFMgrDDNS` | Valid configured/enabled DDNS, verified shared prerequisites and no affecting quarantine. Off removes owned jobs/blocks; On restores. Manual execution uses the shared IPv4/IPv6 resolver policy; missing logging never blocks DNS. |
| New DDNS due-work dispatch (only if needed) | None in reference; exact cadence Pending P1 | Shared native dispatcher or one owned `CFMgrDDNSRetry` | Valid On pending unsatisfied/throttled work receives due dispatch around proposed five-minute fallback, without waiting for daily job. Existing shared periodic job preferred where sufficient; never duplicate jobs/IP writes or retry before valid server delay. Off/auth-Off removes any exact own retry job; no manager/backup cron. |
| `CloudflareAccessIP` | `*/5 * * * *` | `CFMgrAccessRetry` | Valid configured/enabled IP-Sync, verified shared prerequisites and no affecting quarantine. Off removes owned jobs/observation blocks; On restores. No pending work means no API call; missing logging never blocks sync. |
| New shared native discovery | Proposed `*/5 * * * *` | `CFMgrPublicIP` | One coalesced job while either native feature is valid/On. Refresh current family states and reconcile configured remote targets even unchanged IP; detect disable/absence and remove inactive entries. Reuse eligible current DDNS observations, standalone IP-Sync needs no DNS. Off/status stay local; no extra daemon/job. |
| `scribeLogRotate` (supplemental snapshot) | `5 0 * * *` | Keep Scribe's existing job | Scribe owns its own rotation schedule. CFMgr adds a policy, not another rotation cron. |

Remove old Cloudflare jobs only after their commands/ownership are recognized and the new configuration is committed. In particular, the generic name `UpdateDDNS` alone is insufficient proof of ownership. Never remove BackupMon or unrelated jobs. Existing Cloudflare schedule defaults are retained unless explicitly changed in configuration.

CFMgr backups are manual only. Restore/repair rebuilds current-code integrations from activation flags, never archived script bodies/commands. Explicit Cloudflared Off removes ALL owned runtime/start/WAN/mount/watchdog/updater/service blocks/jobs; installed files and update preference remain. Later explicit maintenance-only installation can activate only maintenance. Healthy repair follows these flags; corruption or uninstall/reset intent suppresses affected execution/reconstruction until resolved. Turning off automatic binary updates removes only its updater job, preserving tunnel/watchdog activation.

Cron dispatch permission follows health/generation/activation, scoped provider retry_due and feature dependencies. Saved eligible jobs remain across storage/network/throttle waits, never auth-Off. Cloudflared/updater defer promptly until actual mount; native jobs stay independent of Entware/custom logs. Due dispatcher coalesces newest work without defeating server delay; matching mount resumes only eligible scope, no blind enable/start or boot wait. Auth-Off records fault then removes affected operational integrations under shared guard, preserving logging rotation independently.

### Boot safety and bounded automatic work

R35 applies to every automatic entry point and its descendants, including failed DDNS/IP-Sync admission when Entware is unavailable. Router boot and normal firmware operation must proceed when CFMgr cannot start or publish. This is a required implementation/acceptance contract, not a claim that the current skeleton has passed boot tests. Merlin's [hook documentation](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/wiki/User-scripts) distinguishes blocking hooks, including unmount; its firmware timeout is an outer limit, never a CFMgr wait budget. Exact supported-firmware hook and DDNS callback timing remains a P1.19 gate.

| Boundary | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Foreground hook | Only bounded local validation, eligible job registration and nonblocking admission/dispatch, plus bounded owned-process teardown control for unmount/services-stop within their verified deadlines. The explicitly selected DDNS completion wait below is the only publishing exception. Other hooks perform no foreground network, mount/NTP/DNS wait, WAN settle sleep, download, interactive prompt or recovery loop. Freeze and measure a maximum CFMgr contribution for each hook, including config parsing, locks, cron tooling and logging; reject a design that relies on the firmware killing it at timeout. Missing/busy/broken prerequisites defer promptly. CFMgr failure cannot exit a populated foreign hook early, skip later add-ons or replace their completion/result. |
| Detachment and descriptors | Before long work, detach hook stdin/stdout/stderr and any inherited output pipes/terminal; use safe bounded sinks that do not require Entware. Backgrounding with `&` alone is not evidence of independence. Close unrelated hook/controller descriptors; preserve only deliberately required provider-writer exclusion until its in-flight child is quiescent under D47/V59. Verify hook exit and output-channel closure while the worker remains alive. |
| Admission, locks and resources | Coalesce before spawning a worker or settle sleeper, with at most one active worker and one bounded newest pending request per scope. Bound total workers/children/timers, RAM queue/cache/staging size, child lifetime and notices across scopes. Busy locks never wait in a hook, kill the holder or unlink a live lock. Failed fork/low RAM/full JFFS/read-only storage cannot recurse, spin, fill logs or leave a growing population of contenders. |
| Worker deadline and retry | Bound the entire action plus owned-child cleanup, not only individual curl/ping commands. Native discovery/publishing, startup, maintenance and recovery share scoped admission/backoff/due-time rules; repeated events and DDNS callbacks cannot bypass those rules. No immediate self-respawn, nested dispatch loop or retry-until-success worker. Exit/defer pending work to an eligible later dispatcher without keeping a boot caller alive. Recheck current config/family/generation before retry. |
| Waiting is an outage state | Reuse the startup/outage readiness contract below for cold boot, late mount/NTP, Internet loss and dropped tunnels. A dispatch/watchdog may check prerequisites and retain pending intent, but cannot start/restart a connector, publish changes or begin a network update while any required gate is false or unknown. Wait means exit promptly and retry later under backoff, not skip readiness or keep boot waiting. Safe local status/Off/guard/cleanup and prerequisite checks remain available; optional custom logging cannot block otherwise ready native providers. |
| DDNS completion | User selected a short, strictly capped DDNS hook wait so verified success/failure is reported to Merlin before that invocation returns. This explicit exception supersedes immediate-return requirements for DDNS only. Prove one completion owner, current invocation/overlap handling, a whole-action deadline including cleanup/callback, timeout failure, cooldown, lock contention and firmware retry/reentry. No stale detached callback or invented success. Busy/missing prerequisites fail/defer promptly, never wait for mount/NTP recovery. IP-Sync observation/queue delivery is independent and cannot hold DDNS completion open. Exact duration and supported overlap semantics remain P1.19 implementation gates. |
| Failed launch and recovery | Transient failures retain capped retries. Proven invalid config, incompatible binary or missing essential capability leaves affected execution safely inactive/repair-required until relevant state changes or explicit repair; do not repeatedly download, rewrite or restart it. Repeated unexplained early crashes retain capped launch-failure backoff; the reference two-second survival check cannot reset that history. Require a declared sustained-stability criterion before reset, observed by existing bounded dispatchers without another daemon. This does not add release pin/blacklist/skip-version controls or infer auth-Off from a crash. |
| Boot/mount recovery and teardown | Boot/mount may assess local guards and enqueue eligible bounded recovery; never enter a wizard/login/create/reinstall loop or repeatedly activate an unresolved update. Preserve pending/old-generation evidence and defer uncertain recovery. Establish shutdown/unmount suppression before stopping owned work; cancel pending admission, bound child cleanup within the verified hook budget and prevent late workers/watchdogs from restarting during teardown. Resume only under a later valid lifecycle/mount event. |
| No firmware restart recovery | No automatic reboot, poweroff, reboot scheduling, global service/WAN/DNS restart, firmware-watchdog manipulation or recursive invocation of firmware hooks to recover CFMgr. Lifecycle control is limited to verified CFMgr-owned processes. A CFMgr fault must leave routing, firmware startup and unrelated services able to proceed; persistent inability to recover is reported as waiting/repair-required rather than escalated to router restart. |

P1.19 freezes concrete foreground/worker/resource/stability limits and the native mechanisms that enforce them before dependent code. P3/P4/P5 implement this contract; V73/P8 verify it with all features enabled and fault injection, including no Entware, dead network, busy locks, hung children, event storms, failed startup, corrupt recovery state and output-pipe capture. Observe boot return time, peak worker/resource counts and retry spacing; intercept and fail any attempted reboot/global service restart. Live boot/reboot testing requires separate router authorization. No claim of boot safety is complete from source review or a shell syntax check alone.

**DDNS clarification on resume:** the capped completion wait must never hold up boot or wait for the router, mount, NTP or Internet to become ready. Early calls defer promptly. The additional CFMgr-forced retry is triggered **only by a failed/timed-out firmware DDNS service attempt**; successful attempts create no extra update. Retain one coalesced pending retry, then use the normal current-config/address update/readback path after all required readiness gates pass. Apply bounded attempts/backoff/provider cooldowns, cancel after Off/config invalidation, and preserve auth/config repair guards. Never force the callback to success without a verified result. This does not add an unconditional forced-update loop or change the existing ordinary publishing schedules. The reference's 5-second connect/20-second per-HTTP-request limits are not an aggregate DDNS deadline; the final whole-action/cleanup/callback cap still needs implementation tests.

## Installation layout and Entware checks

The normal core/DDNS/IP-Sync operational profile uses POSIX shell, verified firmware tools and the small shared Entware dependency set in R36/D55. A native entry/recovery path must still report missing prerequisites without blocking router hooks. Keep persistent small settings/credentials/ownership/recovery in JFFS, frequent state/queues in RAM, and Entware packages/binary/tunnel/log data on the verified mount. The current tunnel setup uses `/opt/bin/cloudflared` and `/opt/etc/cloudflared`; DDNS/IP-Sync do not depend on the tunnel's data or activation.

CFMgr's settings file will be `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/config`, following the add-on convention used by [vpnmgr](https://github.com/h0me5k1n/vpnmgr/blob/main/vpnmgr.sh) and [Scribe](https://github.com/AMTM-OSR/scribe/blob/master/scribe.sh). CFMgr's small settings/helper files share this dedicated directory. Keep the complete original validated account certificate at `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/cert.pem` with private mode and account/format/provenance/hash/generation metadata. This is a small recovery copy, not renewed authentication. Capture it on successful reviewed login/import/repair certificate replacement; seed a missing copy only from a trusted validated certificate during explicit schema migration/reinstall. A matching copy causes no duplicate JFFS write. Prove practical native certificate size/space bounds and fail-safe behavior; typical certificate size is not a fixed acceptance limit. Keep full user YAML only on Entware and preserve it on KEEP. Merlin documents `/jffs/configs` for firmware configuration overrides; CFMgr's own settings are read by the manager. See [Merlin custom config files](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/wiki/Custom-config-files).

### Selected dependency policy

| Component | Initial dependency rule |
| --- | --- |
| Base installer | Fresh install is passive JFFS manager/helpers/default config after capability/space and required-package checks. First detect retained-install marker/data; valid retained setup follows current-code reconstruction. Manager `u`/`uf` also checks/repairs dependencies without reinstalling feature daemons/hooks/workers. |
| Manager, menus, DDNS, and IP-Sync | BusyBox-compatible POSIX sh, verified firmware curl/CA/utilities and shared jq/coreutils-timeout/coreutils-sha256sum. jq selection/serialization uses the ordinary non-regex package; retain strict input/schema/duplicate/size handling rather than regex-only success matching. Native recovery diagnostics do not require working jq. Use explicit verified command paths and isolated library/CA environment. No router Python/venv/picker. |
| Tunnel / binary maintenance | Official cloudflare/cloudflared direct release download remains selected, not opkg cloudflared. Shared prerequisites plus bind-dig where native DNS cannot satisfy SRV/A readiness; only tunnel scope installs this DNS package. Existing explicit setup/install/recovery/update eligibility is unchanged. |
| Backup/restore | Prefer stock tar/gzip and firmware integrity-check utilities. Add only an essential missing backup/restore capability if the target firmware requires it; document that minimum explicitly before declaring support. |
| Entware | Required for shared operational packages and selected tunnel/custom-logging work. CFMgr installs/checks its selected packages, not Entware itself. Prefer usable native flock; add the flock package only where needed. Missing shared requirements stop operational work, with bounded next-launch retry and native recovery access. |
| Scribe logging | Optional custom file logging requires actually mounted writable Entware plus configured Scribe/logrotate. Cloudflared policy is editable; DDNS/IP-Sync policies fixed. Missing storage/Scribe disables effective file output/check/repair without changing saved preferences or blocking API work. Native logger/system messages need neither Entware nor Scribe. |
| Picker | Native shell menus accept numeric and text choices; mounted-drive/archive entries remain numbered with `e` for Back/cancel. `fzf` is an undecided future route, outside initial implementation/dependency scope. |

| Location | Proposed contents | Storage/permissions |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `/jffs/scripts/cfmgr` | Main menu/CLI entry point. | Small executable, mode `755`; POSIX `sh`. |
| `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/` | Small manager settings, bundled private helpers and non-secret ownership/install metadata. | CFMgr-owned JFFS directory; no large tunnel files/log history/archives. Preserve shared parent permissions. |
| `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/common.sh` (proposed filename) | Shared POSIX shell library loaded by the main script/owned noninteractive handlers, including DDNS/IP-Sync and Cloudflared readiness/outage/dispatch/lock helpers. | Verified package code on JFFS, independent of Entware; no credentials or startup/network/mutation side effects from sourcing. Exact helper split/filename frozen in P1/P2. |
| `/opt/bin/cloudflared` | Installed binary, with protected `.old` and pending-update companion files. | Entware drive; executable validated for this router. |
| `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/config` | Versioned settings and authoritative small secrets: connector token, DDNS API credential and IP-Sync API token/account/zone/list/record settings. | JFFS, root-owned `600`, non-executable safe parsing. No required DDNS/IP-Sync credential path under `/opt`; imported legacy values migrate privately only at Save. |
| `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/setup-drafts` | Versioned DDNS/IP-Sync draft fields, private candidate credential/account/zone/list data, validation flags/base revision and next stage. | JFFS root-owned 600, safe data parsing/atomic bounded checkpoints after completed input, never each keystroke. Inert/separate from active credentials/config; e/Ctrl+C retain completed progress. Included as sensitive inert data in authorized backups/KEEP; owned WIPE/reset removes it. |
| `/opt/etc/cloudflared/config.yml` | Advanced runtime YAML, imported in B-y or generated with UUID/credential path and hostless/commented examples in B-n. | Entware; mode `600`. Fresh B warns before replacing existing selected files at Save and retains private recovery copies. Normal import/repair/reinstall does not regenerate application settings. Full uninstall KEEP preserves this primary file; WIPE/reset removes only the owned file. |
| `/opt/etc/cloudflared/import/` | Private incoming advanced YAML/JSON/certificate location shown by B-y when files need placing. | Preparation may create the folder, but copies stay separate from a working configuration until reviewed import/Save. Never infer activation from folder/file presence. |
| `/opt/etc/cloudflared/credentials/` | Derived private runtime JSON, selected administrative cert from supported login/import and other tunnel data. | Private modes/validated account/path association. Full small runtime authority in main config; preserve original account-cert bytes in the owned JFFS copy and include both cert copies in coherent data backup. Preserve user application YAML/foreign files; KEEP preserves primary runtime files, WIPE/reset removes owned files. |
| `/opt/etc/cloudflared/config.yml.bak` | Private CFMgr-owned previous application YAML retained during explicit replacement for ingress/manual recovery. | Record ownership/hash/generation; do not overwrite/adopt a foreign .bak. Preserve known-good original/recovery until commit; backup includes .bak as inert data. Full uninstall KEEP retains the owned copy; WIPE/reset remove it under D45. Missing/read-only mount defers required cleanup. |
| `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/recovery/` | Bounded durable pre-change core config/helper/owned-hook copies and small transaction metadata. | Private `700`/`600`; keeps recovery data available without Entware; dependent operations still enforce R36/D55. Write on explicit changes only, exclude from user archives, preserve unresolved copies. Bound space before Save. |
| `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/cert.pem` | Original-byte account-certificate recovery copy beside master config, only when a selected trusted certificate exists. | Private mode `600`, account/format/provenance/hash/generation association; practical size/space bound Pending. Coherent data backup includes it; missing runtime cert can recover from it without new login. KEEP preserves it, WIPE/reset removes owned copy; no full user-YAML JFFS duplicate. |
| `/opt/var/lib/cfmgr/` | Cloudflared binary-install/update/provenance and compact persistent tunnel observations. | Used only for selected Cloudflared work on mounted Entware; no manager/DDNS/IP-Sync runtime dependency. |
| `/opt/var/log/cfmgr/` | Separate `cloudflared.log`, `ddns.log`, `ip-sync.log`, and their owned rotations. | Shared directory, created/used only when a configured feature's custom logging is on and Scribe is ready. Each feature owns only its file/rule; no separate manager logfile. |
| `<selected-mount>/CFBackup/` | User-created data-only CFMgr `.tar.gz` backups. | At the root of a drive explicitly selected from mounted storage; private directory `700`, archives `600` where supported. Never use an unmounted leftover directory. |
| `/opt/var/tmp/cfmgr/` | Larger Cloudflared/tunnel download/restore staging and recovery copies. | Requires mounted Entware only for that scope; no default core staging dependency. Final archive staging stays on selected backup filesystem for atomic rename. |
| `/tmp/cfmgr/` | Private native queue/locks/staging, confirmed target desired-state cache, scoped retry_due, bounded Cloudflared failure/status evidence/readiness/PID and derived token handoff. | RAM private/no required /opt. No persistent raw debug/syslog stream or polling daemon. Boot/restore invalidates live success/queues/cooldown authority; compact historical observations/advisory fault data only may be snapshotted, no execution-state import. |
| `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/setup-state` | Independently readable non-secret generation/recovery and install/uninstall/reset guard; bounded retained-install identity/cleanup metadata. | Private persistent intent written before active mutations; suppress execution/rebuild while removal/reset incomplete even if old config says On. Retained completed removal is distinct from interruption; no archive export. Exact schema/bounds are P1 work. |
| `/opt/etc/logrotate.d/` | Separate owned Cloudflared/DDNS/IP-Sync rotation fragments when Scribe is usable. | Preserve unrelated/global rules. Direct feature files need no new syslog-ng route or extra rotation schedule. |

`/opt` is the application-facing Entware namespace; discover its mounted target without hardcoding a drive label. Bare `/etc` or an unmounted `/opt` is never a fallback for tunnel/log data. Core manager/DDNS/IP-Sync configuration, small durable recovery and RAM state are intentionally outside Entware. User archives may be on any selected mounted drive, including a drive without Entware. [Merlin's Entware guide](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/wiki/Entware) describes the required Entware installation.

### Firmware-native DDNS/IP-Sync feasibility

| Evidence | Planning conclusion / proof still required |
| --- | --- |
| Supplied DDNS code | Shell/curl/grep/logger and Merlin completion; no `/opt` paths found, but DNS record enumeration AND optional IP-Sync notification invoke Python. Replace both paths; retain DNS semantics and strengthen actual JSON validation. |
| Supplied IP-Sync code | Python stdlib JSON/IP/fcntl plus curl and RAM queue; reference manages one public IPv4. Shell rewrite retains transport/queue/target/readback safety and deliberately adopts authoritative current-family reconciliation, remote-drift overwrite and empty-list removal. No Python wrapper/fallback; mixed/empty/native paths need proof. |
| [Merlin custom DDNS docs](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/wiki/Custom-DDNS) and [firmware build](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/main/release/src/router/Makefile) | Documented JFFS hook/WAN argument/completion; build installs curl to `/usr/sbin/curl` and configures firmware CA bundle `/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt`. Verify exact installed model/release/options and HTTPS trust before support. Never add `-k` or rely on Entware CA/curl. |
| [Firmware BusyBox base config](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/main/release/src/router/busybox/config_base) | Enables awk/sed/flock/logger, supporting a native route; it does not prove a suitable JSON CLI or every temporary-file applet exists on installed firmware. Validate actual native tools and use a proven bundled shell/awk parser/serializer and safe private staging as needed. No router Python fallback; selected jq/opkg use follows R36/D55. |

This establishes source-level feasibility, not a completed port or live-router proof. P1/P4 must test native JSON correctness, IPv4/IPv6 edge cases, CA/HTTPS/timeout/response-size handling, locking and atomic queue/state writes with approved native/Entware resolution and explicit missing/poisoned dependency cases. Native recovery must work with `/opt` absent. Library presence alone is not a usable JSON command. No frequent status/queue/log writes to JFFS; no custom files without mounted Entware/Scribe/logrotate. Firmware-native system messages remain independent.

### Required preflight

1. For every action confirm supported Merlin/root/JFFS user scripts, safely parsed config/health, native commands and sufficient space in the affected JFFS/RAM scope. Operational work also verifies the shared R36/D55 prerequisites; native recovery/status and safe Off/removal must remain available when these fail.
2. Resolve native curl/utilities/trust independently of inherited PATH, loader or CA overrides. Execute Entware tools only through approved paths after storage/ABI/capability checks. No TLS bypass or integrity waiver; document every selected dependency.
3. Resolve `/opt` symlinks and actual mount identity before Entware execution or mutation. A directory/symlink/executable alone is insufficient; require the expected persistent mount, not rootfs/JFFS/tmpfs, with writable storage for actions that write. Freeze retained-volume and mount-loss handling before implementing package mutation.
4. Cloudflared itself comes from a checked official release, independent of an opkg cloudflared package. Its installation still requires the shared tools, verified persistent Entware storage, supported architecture/ABI, official asset digest and verified HTTPS/metadata handling. Check scope space/permissions before staging/extraction and retain/recheck the expected mount throughout writes; never use a disappeared drive's leftover mountpoint.
5. Recheck the expected Entware volume before every dependent stage and during owned-child supervision. If it vanishes, boundedly interrupt/reap only this action's owned child, retain incomplete guard/recoverable evidence and report unavailable storage. For an in-flight tunnel create, warn a failed tunnel may exist and direct manual Zero Trust inspection/deletion before explicit retry after the same expected mount is verified again. Do not wait in boot/menu, write to a leftover mountpoint or automatically repeat creation. Native recovery/status, e, public Off/unhook and safe owned stop remain available from JFFS/RAM.
