| Service Off / Stop | No new owned service/API events; preserve logging preference, files/rotations/rules. Scribe/logrotate continues independent rotation, with no CFMgr stop/start/restart of its daemon/job/global policies. Logging checks cannot enable the service. |
| Scribe becomes unavailable | Effective file output unavailable; preserve requested preference/history and native messages. Coordinate an eligible owned Cloudflared controller restart without logfile or quiesce native job writer as needed; no invented native daemon/forced sync. A still-running writer is not proof of ready Scribe. Explicit Uninstall logging works immediately without re-detect/heal, follows cleanup contract below and saves opt-out. |
| Classified provider outcome / auth-Off notice | Redacted throttle/auth notices go native and to that feature's installed/requested/ready file, including final classified auth-Off diagnostic before or as quiescence commits; never start service or enable logging to write notice. Logging uninstall guard/opt-out suppresses file writes. Scribe rotation remains independent of service enable. Bounded RAM runtime evidence/classifier works without Scribe, not raw log/history capture. |

Custom-log readiness requires verified Entware, installed/configured Scribe, usable syslog-ng/logrotate executables and base configurations, active syslog-ng as required by Scribe, a functioning Scribe rotation schedule, and validated inclusion of owned fragments. Probe the base prerequisites separately from generated files so missing owned rules are repairable. Native messages need no Scribe-readiness check. Detection is read-only; repair does not reinstall Scribe or replace global configuration.

The captured Scribe reinstall calls uninstall before install (lines 3106–3119); uninstall removes `/opt/etc/logrotate.conf` and the entire fragment directory (lines 2151–2161). Scribe also regenerates `A01global` (lines 781–800). Keep CFMgr preferences/cloudflared policy in JFFS and versioned fixed-policy metadata with the manager, then generate separate owned fragments after completed reinstall instead of relying on surviving files or editing `A01global`.

### Proposed owned logging integration

| Item | Planned behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Files and routing | `/opt/var/log/cfmgr/cloudflared.log`, `ddns.log`, and `ip-sync.log` are separate owned files. Detailed cloudflared output stays file-only. DDNS/IP-Sync redacted events also go to native messages; brief manager maintenance/readiness notices stay native. No separate manager file or new syslog-ng routes. |
| Cloudflared file | Retain/migrate existing application log and rotations with recoverable staging. Use validated daemon logfile arguments, not Scribe auto-discovery or a pipe into syslog. |
| DDNS/IP-Sync event files | Append bounded structured lines containing time, feature/target identity, outcome, and verified synced IP where relevant. Never log tokens, certificates, credential bodies, or full API responses. Serialize owned writes; short-lived append/open/close avoids assuming daemon-style reopen semantics. Logging errors do not fail API work or Merlin completion. |
| Rotation ownership | One non-A01global fragment per feature under included logrotate.d; Scribe alone manages scheduler/rotation. Service Stop/Off retains its rule/history and independent rotation. Explicit logging uninstall removes only selected owned setup/data; no Scribe daemon/job/global-policy control or deletion of another feature's rule/shared history. |
| Cloudflared policy | Generate from validated main-config settings below and explicitly override inherited policy where needed. |
| DDNS/IP-Sync policy | Generate each fixed size-based policy from the manager's versioned defaults below. No custom size/count/time/compression options in their menus or editable schema; save preference/policy version, not user overrides. Status can show the actual rule. |
| Open-file handling | Verify cloudflared log-reopen behavior before selecting rename/reopen or `copytruncate`; document the small loss window if copytruncate is required. A syslog-ng HUP does not prove the daemon reopened its file. Validate concurrent short-lived DDNS/IP-Sync append/rotation behavior separately. |
| Validation and backup | Validate the active include chain in logrotate debug mode during setup/repair, including duplicate/nested matches. Agree a non-deadlocking writer/rotation/snapshot strategy for eligible owned log backups; never include the whole router syslog. |

### Rotation settings in the main configuration

These are proposed cloudflared setting names/defaults for `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/config`; serialization and installed-tool support remain P1/P6 work. Apply basic size-based rotation only when first fresh cloudflared setup completes with ready Scribe, whether token or advanced mode. Keep size/count/time/age/compression tuning in the Cloudflared logging submenu, available with configured tunnel and ready Scribe; do not ask those advanced questions in initial tunnel setup. Preserve imported/saved policy and later user changes. These tuning fields never control DDNS/IP-Sync or native system messages. The supplied launch helper sets a logfile but contains no size threshold, and active rotation rules are absent from that snapshot; `10M` below remains proposed until current-policy evidence confirms the desired size.

| Setting | Proposed fresh-setup default | User option / behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `logging_enabled` | On for first cloudflared setup with ready Scribe; otherwise off | Saved custom-file installation/output preference, independent of service On/Off and Scribe's rotation schedule. Uninstall logging saves false; explicit reviewed Install/enable needed after opt-out. Keep readiness separate. |
| `logrotate_mode` | Size | Size-based rotation, or optional time-based rotation with a size threshold. |
| `logrotate_size` | `10M` | Validated positive threshold with units; required for size mode. |
| `logrotate_keep` | `5` | Positive bounded rotated-file count, excluding the live log; remove oldest when exceeded. |
| `logrotate_interval` | Unset in size mode | Optional daily/weekly/monthly time policy; cannot increase Scribe's actual run frequency. |
| `logrotate_compress` | On | Compression on/off using available gzip support. |
| `logrotate_delay_compress` | On | With compression, optionally leave newest rotation uncompressed until the next cycle. |
| `logrotate_max_age_days` | Off | Optional positive age limit; count retention works independently. |

### Fixed DDNS and IP-Sync policies

The fixed policy is shared in shape but generated separately for `ddns.log` and `ip-sync.log`. No rotation tuning prompts/menu/config overrides. Numeric defaults below are proposals to settle in P1/P6, not user-selected values or implemented guarantees.

| Fixed setting | Proposed value / behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Size trigger | `1M` for each file; positive fixed threshold, evaluated at Scribe's cadence. |
| History | Keep one numbered rotated file plus the live file; purge the oldest owned rotation. |
| Compression/time/age | Uncompressed, size-based only, no separate time/age policy or schedule. |
| File handling | Skip absent/empty files; preserve private permissions. Verify append/rotation concurrency before implementation. |
| Preferences | Separate proposed `ddns_logging_enabled` / `ip_sync_logging_enabled` and fixed-policy version/readiness. Auto-initialize on first fresh setup with ready Scribe; re-detect/enable validates the same defaults. No Scribe means no custom event file, while native messages continue. |

All three use numbered history: `cloudflared.log.1` through `.N` (and `.gz` when compressed), or the fixed `ddns.log.1` / `ip-sync.log.1`. Generate `nodateext`/`start 1` rather than inheriting date names. Size-only maps to `size`; cloudflared time-plus-size maps to the interval with `maxsize`. Use `missingok`/`notifempty`; verify directive support against installed logrotate, following the [upstream manual](https://github.com/logrotate/logrotate/blob/main/logrotate.8.in).

A size threshold is checked when Scribe runs; it is not an immediate hard cap. Show actual check cadence beside the policy. No CFMgr polling/rotation daemon, second cron, or manager-launch truncation. Altering Scribe's global schedule/system-message retention is outside initial scope.

### Fast check, repair, and explicit logging installation/removal

| Trigger/state | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Healthy manager launch | Check small saved state and owned files/fingerprints/include/prerequisite signals only for configured features with custom logging requested. Share base probes but retain per-feature results. No full dry-run, rotation, network check, dependency install, retry sleep, or restart. |
| No configured feature requesting custom logs | Skip automatic Scribe/rule checks entirely. Status/native messages still work as available. |
| Missing/stale owned fragment/include | Only for requested logging with usable Scribe and no cleanup guard, regenerate affected owned integration from saved/versioned policy; validate/commit atomically. Never recreate missing Scribe base or override opt-out; bounded work defers with notice. |
| Successful self-repair | Name the affected feature/rule, for example `Restored CFMgr DDNS logrotate rule.` Claim success only after validation/commit; deferred, unavailable, and failed are distinct. |
| Custom logging off | Skip automatic checks/repair for that feature. Its explicit re-detect is read-only: report availability and offer explicit enable without silently switching it on. Native messages stay active. |
| Explicit Install/enable logging | Configured feature only; review enable, validate policy/Scribe/storage/owned integration and resolve pending cleanup before activation. Re-detect may offer this action but cannot silently perform it. Never enable the service/other feature's logs or change Scribe runtime/schedule. |
| Explicit Uninstall logging | Available in selected configured feature submenu even when Scribe absent or unusable; save preference inactive and suppress writer/self-heal under shared guard before cleanup below. No prerequisite re-detect/heal or separate runtime logging-stop UI. |
| Explicit re-detect with logging on | Inside configured submenu run bounded complete validation/repair even if cache ready; preserve preference and report outcome. Never turn Off feature On. |
| Cloudflared policy change | Validate/stage/dry-run/commit config and owned rule together with recovery copies. Invalid changes preserve working policy. DDNS/IP-Sync expose no equivalent custom tuning. |
| `0) Check status` | Read and report enabled-feature observations/policy state only; do not run repair or full validation. Disabled features receive no runtime/logging details or probes. |

Proposed launch budget remains under one second when healthy, with at most two seconds of foreground check/repair across all three integrations; measure in P8. Use nonblocking locks and bounded validation; contention defers instead of waiting. Background hooks never prompt. Cache only results tied to current settings/files; missing/changed files invalidate the cache. No additional periodic repair job.

**Repair boundary:** only owned feature fragments, permissions, and include registration. For a valid top-level config missing coverage, add marked includes for the exact owned fragments after duplicate/directory/nested include checks and merged validation. Preserve other text/rules; do not edit `A01global` or native routing/retention. Missing/invalid Scribe base configuration/executables is unavailable, not something to reconstruct from guesses.

**Install/repair transaction:** inspect prerequisites/ownership, take documented nonblocking locks (including Scribe rotation lock only where relevant), retain recovery copies, generate/validate/commit. Only eligible current On/running Cloudflared may need controller restart when logfile arguments change; rule-only/native writer-policy changes need no tunnel restart or forced provider sync. Preserve service intent, other rules/native messages; failed install/repair rolls back safely. No automatic user archive or Scribe daemon/job/global-policy change.

### Per-feature Uninstall logging transaction

| Step / edge condition | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Reviewed intent first | After explicit review/Save, write scoped durable logging-cleanup transaction/generation guard and inactive logging preference BEFORE removing files/rules. Guard prevents watchdog/updater/start handlers respawning the writer during cleanup; suppress self-heal/new writes and quiesce old/inflight writers. Preserve configured/enabled/maintenance flags, policy values and operational hooks/jobs. Temporary stop is INTERNAL controller quiescence, never public cfmgr stop/Off handler; only owned LOG files/rules are removed. Failed/interrupted cleanup never rolls preference active or silently recreates logs. |
| Quiesce selected writer before deletion | A current verified owned Cloudflared process may still hold its open logfile despite saved opt-out/new launch arguments. Explicit cleanup authorizes bounded owned stop BEFORE deletion even if readiness fails or Scribe absent; this is an exception to readiness-before-ordinary-restart. Off is never enabled/started. Native jobs quiesce only owned append worker/inflight writer around deletion, no invented daemon/forced provider sync. Failed stop means no open-log deletion or false stop-success claim. |
| Found owned logs / rotations | Verify actual mounted writable Entware/resolved owned paths, then delete only found owned feature logfile/rotations with bounded inventory and symlink/foreign-root protections. This cleanup does not require Scribe readiness or presence. Preserve other logs/shared directory/native messages/manual archives. |
| Scribe detected, including failed readiness | On mounted writable storage remove independently verified owned rotation fragments that still exist. Edit surviving global include only when exact owned block is identifiable and whole-file merge/syntax/concurrency checks pass; otherwise report residual/deferred cleanup and preserve foreign text. Never create/repair missing Scribe base/includes, edit A01global, or change Scribe daemon/job/schedule/global policy. Its own uninstall may already have removed rules; report found/absent accurately. |
| Scribe fully absent | Skip Scribe config edits/repair/re-detect/heal; inactive preference/writer quiescence and found owned log deletion still proceed. A vanished rule is already absent, never recreated for removal. |
| Resume after safe writer quiescence | Once the old writer is safely quiesced and inactive logging preference durably committed, only still-enabled valid current-generation Cloudflared restarts through existing readiness controller WITHOUT logfile arguments. Readiness/start failure is reported waiting/deferred/failed, not success; saved On remains eligible for gated watchdog recovery without file logging. Native jobs resume eligible existing work without file output, no forced sync. Off remains Off. |
| Missing/read-only/lost Entware or stop failure | Keep saved logging preference/self-heal inactive and durable cleanup metadata; no stale /opt writes or deletion-success claim. Defer unsafe file/rule deletion. Failure to quiesce an existing open writer keeps the temporary execution guard and reports runtime cleanup incomplete, never claimed stopped because preference changed. Attempt verified owned bounded temporary stop independently of Scribe presence; no open-log deletion until safe. Native writers honor inactive preference independent of mount. |
| Temporary execution guard versus pending log cleanup | Hold the scoped execution guard until owned writer quiescence and durable opt-out are verified. Then release that temporary runtime gate while retaining pending file/rule cleanup metadata and inactive logging preference. Residual log cleanup alone cannot block eligible native DDNS/IP-Sync API work or system messages, or ready Cloudflared running without file logging. Report removal incomplete until pending deletion resolves; a pending cleanup record never authorizes file output, self-heal or logging re-enable. |
| Completion / later enable | Report actual writer/rule/file outcomes; release temporary execution gate as above and clear pending cleanup metadata only after required file/rule cleanup safely resolves. Retain opt-out and original operational hooks/jobs/activation intent; ready same intended enabled service resumes without file flags even if safe residual cleanup is pending. Future reviewed Install/enable first resolves pending cleanup and uses scoped Scribe validation; menu/launch/re-detect/restore never overrides inactive choice. Other service integrations/Scribe rotation cron unchanged; rotation always Scribe-owned. |

## Optional uiScribe log viewer

Read-only review on 2026-10-08 used AMTM-OSR uiScribe [v1.4.14](https://github.com/AMTM-OSR/uiScribe/releases/tag/v1.4.14), commit `11b5b7e`. Its [discovery code](https://github.com/AMTM-OSR/uiScribe/blob/v1.4.14/uiScribe.sh#L427) extracts `/opt/var/log` file destinations from preprocessed syslog-ng configuration. Checkbox settings select discovered paths; the script regenerates lists and WebUI links. No external-log registration interface was found. Our direct logfile plus logrotate rule will not appear automatically, and manually inserting generated entries is not durable.

| Route | Planning conclusion |
| --- | --- |
| Current CFMgr direct-file design | Retain it: daemon output remains file-only, separate from brief native notices. uiScribe viewing needs an additional supported discovery mechanism. |
| Existing syslog-ng destination discovery | Can expose a declared destination without changing uiScribe. Using it for direct-file registration would require additional Scribe configuration/validation; do not fake unused destinations or change logging architecture solely to satisfy the viewer. Not selected. |
| Explicit external-log registry | Proposed uiScribe extension: merge an owned persistent allowlist into discovery and reuse viewer/navigation. Requires uiScribe source work or upstream support; no automatic private-file patching, installation, or required CFMgr dependency. |

The [viewer](https://github.com/AMTM-OSR/uiScribe/blob/v1.4.14/Main_LogStatus_Content.asp) uses log basenames and warns about files over 4 MB; the backend can change parent-directory permissions. Any extension needs bounded viewing, unique labels, owned-path checks, private/redacted data, safe clear/rotate actions, and rotation/reinstall tests. Our proposed cloudflared `10M` policy therefore cannot promise full-file WebUI loading; do not silently change it for uiScribe. This is source research only; installed version and live WebUI access remain unverified.

## Backup and restore

Backup setup is optional. Use one data-only manual format/root-level CFBackup/newest 3–5 retention (proposed 5); prune only after verified creation, no schedule/calendar tiers. Any selected mounted drive is valid. In this section, native-only describes a DDNS/IP-Sync/core payload with no tunnel data; it does not waive the selected tool prerequisites in R36/D55. Such payloads use JFFS/RAM plus the backup drive; tunnel data/prepared maintenance reconstruction additionally requires its verified Entware files. Optional custom-log history may be explicitly deferred below; no executable package is restored.

Use gzip-compressed tar (`.tar.gz`) through stock firmware commands. The captured [BackupMon source](https://github.com/ViktorJp/BACKUPMON/blob/main/backupmon.sh) uses `tar -zcf` for creation and `tar -tzf` for verification (captured `jffs/scripts/backupmon.sh`, lines 4570–4575 and 4597–4604). Validate those capabilities on supported firmware before release; do not require ZIP, GNU tar, or BackupMon itself.

### Destination setup and archive picker

| UI/action | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Discover mounted drives | Read the mount table and enumerate usable mounted storage under `/tmp/mnt` (`/mnt` only if it resolves to the same mounted namespace). Show each mounted label and its full mountpoint; do not treat every directory under `mnt` as a mounted drive. |
| Select destination | Display a vertical numbered list: `1`, `2`, `3`, and so on, with `e` for Back/cancel. Require an explicit choice even if only one drive is mounted. Handle spaces/duplicate labels safely; the selected number maps to the exact mount entry. |
| Create backup folder | After the user's selection and mount/writability checks, create or reuse `<selected-mount>/CFBackup/`. It is at the filesystem root, outside the drive's `entware` directory. Preserve existing archives and unrelated contents. |
| Remember selection | Store the chosen mount identity/path in CFMgr settings; recheck the actual mount before each use. A missing or changed drive prompts for re-selection and never falls back to another drive or a bare mountpoint. |
| No usable drives / cancellation | Explain an empty/unusable list and return to the menu. Invalid entries are retried; `e`/Ctrl+C cancel without changing the destination or creating an archive. |
| Select restore source | Let the user use the configured destination or choose another mounted drive, then list recognized CFMgr archives from its `CFBackup` folder. A restore source selection does not silently change the saved backup destination. |
| Select archive | Show a short vertical numbered list of archive filenames/labels, newest first, using the router's local date/time and selected date order. After selection, show manager version, configured/enabled states, verified manifest, and intended changes; then confirm restore. Empty lists and cancellation return cleanly. |
| Initial picker / future idea | Use firmware-native numbered lists for the initial implementation. `fzf` remains an undecided possible future route; do not implement, detect, or install it as part of initial backup/restore. |
| Archive management | Support manual create, list, inspect, verify, and selected-archive deletion. On a verified new backup, enforce `backup_keep` by deleting the oldest recognized completed CFMgr archives in this backup set. Explicit single-archive deletion requires confirmation; preserve unrelated files and internal recovery copies. |

Example mount menu, using placeholder labels:

```text
Select a backup drive:
1) ROUTER_USB  /tmp/mnt/ROUTER_USB  (Entware mounted here)
2) BACKUP_USB  /tmp/mnt/BACKUP_USB
e) Back / Cancel
```

BackupMon's captured mount picker enumerates `/tmp/mnt` and has numbered choices for multiple mounts (lines 2568–2625). Its main-menu `rs` restore action uses the configured primary/secondary source, lists folders, and asks for a typed folder/day name; advanced mode asks for archive filenames before compatibility checks/final confirmation (lines 6677–6895). CFMgr follows its list/select/review/confirm logic with a short numbered archive list, without the calendar folders or whole-router restore scope. This source audit does not establish live router mount availability.

### Archive dates and rolling retention

| Item | Initial behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Date/time source | Use router local time/timezone. Prefer an actual firmware-reported date format when available; verify this in P1. Do not infer date order from timezone/country guesses. If no reliable regional format is exposed, let the user choose day-month-year, month-day-year, or year-month-day during backup setup. |
| Archive filename/label | Use the exact prefix `CFbackup-` with the chosen regional date order and hyphens within date/time. First backup that local day: `CFbackup-08-10-26.tar.gz` for day-month-year or `CFbackup-10-08-26.tar.gz` for month-day-year. Another backup the same day adds `_hh-mm-ss`, for example `CFbackup-08-10-26_21-45-12.tar.gz`. These are examples, not the current router clock. |
| Same-day/collision check | Identify existing same-day archives in the backup set using saved local calendar-date metadata, including after a display-format change. Add the time suffix for subsequent backups; never overwrite an existing filename. An exact timestamp-name collision fails clearly so the manual action can be retried with a distinct time. |
| Sorting/age | Save a numeric creation timestamp/UTC reference, local calendar date, and display-format/timezone metadata in the manifest. Order/prune by saved creation order, never lexicographically by localized names; preserve correctness across date-format changes and daylight-saving repeats. |
| Count | `backup_keep` accepts 3, 4, or 5; proposed default 5. Count completed recognized archives in the current backup set, not staging files, unrelated files, or another set's archives. No per-day/week/month rules. |
| Purge timing | Verify/commit the new archive first, then remove oldest owned archives until the limit is met. Failed/cancelled/full-disk backups never delete a usable old backup to make room. A failed purge reports that the new archive exists but retention cleanup remains pending. |

The restore list needs only numbered archive labels initially; detailed feature/log/compatibility information appears after selection. A local archive filename is a label, not the source of restore state or retention age.

### Backup contents

| Item | Backup scope | Restore rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Manager executable, helpers and Cloudflared binaries/`.old` | Excluded: backups are data-only and restore no executable package or binary version. | Install clean trusted CFMgr first if entry point absent. Current installed code supplies installers/templates and verified ABI binaries; never execute archive code or downgrade manager/binary. |
| Manager configuration and tunnel credentials | Private config/schema/data generation, typed credential values/refs/expected identity, settings/activation/mode/drafts/preferences, plus owned JFFS original-byte cert recovery copy with account/format/provenance/hash/generation. Source auth fault/intent advisory only. | Current-schema restore validates one coherent generation and derives SAME valid runtime JSON/token. Either validated cert copy can reconstruct the other. Full user YAML is archived from Entware and restored without rewriting valid application content; master repairs only its trio. Actual Off stays Off, advisory intent no auto-On/live guard. Never infer scopes/identity or adopt foreign certs from strings/files or trigger login merely because a cert copy is absent. |
| Tunnel configuration data | Inventoried owned `/opt/etc/cloudflared/` YAML/JSON/certificates/inert advanced drafts and eligible legacy data only, under a strict data allowlist, plus the owned JFFS original-byte cert copy; never indiscriminately archive directory contents/code/helpers. Native DDNS/IP-Sync use JFFS credentials and never require this folder. | Restore private JFFS cert copy with the coherent manifest generation; restore/reconstruct Entware runtime files only on verified storage. Preserve full application YAML from backup/current primary and repair master-owned trio from authority. Missing whole YAML requires known-good full recovery/import/repair; no starter fabrication or lost ingress. Presence alone is not configured state. |
| Owned config.yml.bak recovery data | Detect/include present inventoried CFMgr .bak with hash/private modes/role; absent optional .bak is no failure. This is the explicit user recovery file, separate from excluded live transaction journals/copies. | Restore as inactive private YAML recovery data on verified Entware, never auto-apply/derive credentials/enable from it. Validate size/type/path/hash/ownership; preserve foreign .bak and prevent collision takeover. Whole backup restoration includes it, no single-file restore UI. KEEP preserves owned .bak; WIPE/reset remove it. |
| Other owned DNS/IP-Sync settings and credentials | Authoritative JFFS values and inventoried versioned settings data only, no helpers. | Restore JFFS data without tunnel/Python/venv setup or exposing secrets; selected tools follow R36/D55 and current code rebuilds eligible hooks/jobs. |
| Data state and compact historical observations | Mode/activation/preferences/prepared maintenance/prior DDNS settings, bounded historical success/install/update and advisory source fault data. Exclude process ownership, active auth/runtime guards, live last-confirmed cache/cooldowns/retry_due/queues, hooks/jobs/code/recovery guards. | Fresh local ownership/guard/generation and current remote bootstrap; historical last IP never live success/auth. Source repair intent advisory, actual Off no runtime integration/auto-On; maintenance follows activation table. |
| Logging preferences / policy data | Saved logging installation/output preference/opt-out, Cloudflared tuning and fixed-policy data version; exclude executable/generated fragments/includes/policies. | Current templates rebuild requested logging rules/history integration when Scribe/storage usable even for configured service Off; Scribe rotates restored history independently. Runtime On/readiness gates only file writes. Saved logging uninstall/inactive preference suppresses rule self-heal/recreation; explicit Install/enable required, never infer from restored log presence. |
| Owned feature logs and rotated history | Include configured-feature files/rotations only when Entware/Scribe are ready at backup; include eligible disabled-feature history. Native-only archive without logging has none; record included/absent/unavailable per feature. | Log storage requires its verified Entware/Scribe scope; API work separately requires the shared R36/D55 tools. If a native-only archive has optional old logs but no current Entware, explicitly offer core restore with log-history restoration deferred and retain archive; report that history was not restored. Never silently skip required tunnel files or claim complete history restore. Missing Scribe keeps output inactive; whole syslog excluded. |
| Remote Cloudflare resource references | Existing identities/settings needed by configured features; no compulsory API call just to archive local files. | Local restore preserves references and cannot resurrect deleted resources or revoked credentials automatically. |
| Locks, PIDs/process ownership, queues, token handoff, code/hooks/jobs, downloads, active recovery/install guards, staging and backups | Exclude executable/runtime-control content, setup-state, live transaction journals/recovery copies and CFBackup itself. The owned JFFS cert recovery copy is durable data and included above; full YAML is archived from Entware. Explicit inventoried config.yml.bak user recovery file is included separately. | Fresh local restore guard/ownership/generation only; token handoff derives from main config, fresh IP requests use current resolver, never replay stale queues or activate .bak as authority. |
| Unrelated add-on/Entware files, whole hooks, NVRAM, and whole router syslog | Exclude. | Complete CFMgr restore manages its own files/integration points; it does not restore the whole router or overwrite other add-ons. |

`/opt/etc/cloudflared/` is the canonical tunnel-only Entware folder. Accept an imported `/etc/cloudflared` alias only after verified resolution. Include inventoried tunnel files even if disabled; native-only installs do not create/require it. Previously imported DNS/IP-Sync secrets move to authoritative private JFFS settings at reviewed migration Save, without indiscriminate legacy-file deletion. Manifest state determines required files/restore scope and activation.

Backups are native unencrypted archives containing operational credentials/full user YAML/authoritative records. Say plainly in existing review/docs that user is responsible for archive privacy; use best-effort private folder/archive modes and no secret-bearing filenames/arguments. Do not reject a valid mounted writable destination merely because its filesystem cannot enforce POSIX private modes, add encryption dependency or introduce an extra privacy approval gate. Existing safe path/mount/integrity/space checks remain.

### Backup transaction

1. The user explicitly starts a manual backup. If no destination is configured, offer the numbered drive setup; cancellation creates nothing.
2. Verify JFFS/RAM data source and selected backup mount/space/permissions/native tar/gzip. Require Entware source only for inventoried tunnel data or eligible custom history, never to archive a binary/helper. Maintenance-only setup state in JFFS is data; its later reconstruction needs Entware. Native-only backup works on non-Entware drive; recheck mounts before writes, no stale fallback.
3. Acquire documented maintenance/backup locks and verify a coherent committed configuration generation. Defer backup while a setup/update/restore transaction is active, incomplete, or quarantined; offer repair rather than archiving damaged active setup as complete. Capture a consistent owned file set and permissions; coordinate eligible log capture with writers/Scribe rotation without an unnecessary tunnel restart.
4. Data/archive/schema manifest includes chronology/display/backup-set/source manager/generation, mode/activation/preferences, typed credential identity/account/ref data, both owned certificate copies when present with account/format/provenance/hash/generation, full user YAML from Entware, and source modes/log status. Require coherent full runtime YAML and authority for configured tunnel data. Source fault/intent advisory; no live success/cache/cooldown/guards/queues/code versions. Required trusted runtime credential data missing without valid recoverable source fails honestly; absent optional management cert does not invalidate UUID+JSON runtime or initiate login. Absent intentional binary no data-backup failure. Credential content payload only, archive modes/privacy best-effort.
5. Build a uniquely named `.tar.gz` through stock firmware tools, using a private temporary archive on the selected backup filesystem. Store only relative paths in the archive, mapped to approved restore roots by the manifest.
6. Verify archive readability and the manifest/payload hashes, recheck the backup mount, then atomically rename the temporary archive to its final name in `CFBackup`. Report the archive path and included modules/log status only after success.
7. After successful verification/commit, enforce the saved 3–5 archive limit by purging only the oldest recognized completed archives in the same backup set on the verified mount. Keep the new archive and internal recovery material. Full disk, compression failure, mount loss, or cancellation before completion must not delete a usable old backup or silently omit required files; clean only this operation's owned temporary files. Report a retention cleanup failure separately from successful archive creation.

**Internal recovery is separate:** setup replacement, migration, manager updates, logging changes, and restore retain private transaction copies of files/integrations they may replace. These support interruption/failure rollback, do not create archives in `CFBackup`, and require no backup destination setup. Keep unresolved recovery material until the transaction is safely resolved; the binary updater's `.old`/pending recovery contract also remains intact. An additional user backup happens only through an explicit manual action.

### Feature-aware restore rules

Restore runs in the current installed trusted manager. If absent, install clean CFMgr first, then use the same native mount/archive picker. Validate data/archive/schema versions and manifest/config agreement before writes; current code migrates supported older data schemas with recovery. Newer/unsupported schema is rejected with instruction to install a compatible manager. Informational source CFMgr version never selects or downgrades code; never execute/source backup content. Rebuild setup with current normal installers/templates only for archived configured features or explicitly prepared maintenance setup, using the activation table.

Restore includes full user application YAML and authoritative typed credential/expected identity records; derive current private JSON/token handoff without rewriting user content. Historical success/fault/repair-intent metadata is advisory. Keep actual archived Off Off; no imported active auth/runtime guards/queues/cooldowns or auto-On from requested repair intent. Eligible On bootstrap verifies current remote/auth outcome through normal classifier; throttle/network remains On waiting, new definitive rejection persists current local fault/public Off and requires explicit repair.

In restore/reconstruction rules, `unhooked` means no operational service startup/event hooks or cron jobs. Scribe rotation fragments follow saved logging preference and readiness independently; restoring an opted-in rule for an Off feature never starts that service.

| Saved feature state | Files and integrations to restore | Activation after restore |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Never configured, no prepared maintenance | Restore shared settings and any explicitly archived inert advanced draft/data; do not install feature components or fabricate setup. | Unconfigured/Off, no hooks/jobs/start/provider calls. Draft completion still needs reviewed detection/setup. |
| Configured and On | Restore required data; rebuild current-code helpers/normal installers and fresh ownership/eligible hooks/jobs. Cloudflared gets verified current supported ABI binary. | Validate coherent generation/readiness before activation; fresh native IP requests use current shared resolver. No remote recreation. |
| Configured and Off | Restore data/options. Full Cloudflared setup reconstruction installs verified ABI binary/current helpers even Off; no runtime/start/WAN/mount/watchdog integration. Requested installed logging may restore history and rebuild its owned Scribe rotation fragment when storage/Scribe are ready, independently of service activation. | Service remains Off with no operational hooks/jobs. Updater integration only if independently archived maintenance_enabled=true and saved update preference On (a later explicit maintenance action); preference alone never activates. Scribe can rotate restored history without starting the feature. |
| Prepared Cloudflared maintenance, no completed tunnel | Rebuild current verified binary/helpers from recorded prepared maintenance state; preserve any draft inertly. Never synthesize token/YAML/tunnel completion. | Updater only for maintenance_enabled+auto-update preference; no tunnel runtime/watchdog/logging setup. Inactive prepared maintenance remains unhooked. |

Saved activation, not update preference or file presence, governs operational integrations. An explicit Off archive has enabled=false and maintenance_enabled=false; its reconstructed binary/data has no service hooks/jobs. Separately enabled maintenance-only activation is the sole Off-tunnel updater exception. Native Off retains settings without operational blocks/jobs. Owned Scribe rotation fragments follow saved logging preference/readiness independently, including for configured Off features with restored history; logging opt-out receives no rule recreation. Inert drafts/installed maintenance never imply completed/enabled tunnel. Invalid archive fails before writes; interrupted restore remains guarded until complete validated generation.

| Example backup | Required restore result |
| --- | --- |
| Only DDNS was ever set up | Restore DDNS/shared data; current code rebuilds eligible native integration only. No Cloudflared or IP-Sync install/setup. |
| All three configured; only DDNS On, Cloudflared explicitly Off | Restore all data; current installer reconstructs Cloudflared binary/helpers but no tunnel/updater hooks/jobs. Activate DDNS only; no IP-Sync operational integration. Preserve saved update preference dormant. |
| Tunnel configured Off; later maintenance-only install enabled updater | Restore selected method/data/current verified binary/helpers; no tunnel runtime/watchdog. Updater only for independently saved maintenance_enabled+update preference. No native feature setup. |
| Only DDNS or IP-Sync was configured with eligible owned logs | Restore that feature's settings/status/log history and fixed policy, gated on Scribe/readiness and saved activation choices. No tunnel install/files or cloudflared-specific logging setup. |

When restoring over a different existing CFMgr setup, remove/deactivate only stale CFMgr-owned integrations for features absent from the chosen backup and stop a superseded owned connector when necessary. Preserve unrelated add-ons and files; do not purge old data merely because a feature is absent. Missing required payload/configuration is a restore validation failure, not a reason to invent new Cloudflare resources or silently change configured/enabled flags.

### Restore transaction

1. In current trusted CFMgr select drive/archive; verify source mount/integrity/data-schema/manifest-config agreement and current-code migration compatibility before writes. Newer/unsupported data requires compatible manager, never archive code. Bound JFFS/RAM and verify target ABI; required tunnel/maintenance reconstruction needs Entware, core payloads do not require tunnel storage, but still enforce their selected tool prerequisites. Review optional deferred log history before Save.
2. Inspect entries, then extract into bounded private scope-appropriate staging: RAM/JFFS core recovery for native payload; verified Entware for large tunnel/log payload. Strict allowlist/size bounds reject absolute/traversal paths, unsafe links/devices/conflicting duplicates or writes outside roots. Validate hashes/modes before commit.
3. Show configured/enabled/maintenance activation, data schema/migration, current-code reconstruction, eligible logs/paths/integration for review. Resolve storage/backup destination explicitly; never recreate another router's mount label automatically.
4. After reviewed Save, acquire maintenance/update/lifecycle locks in the documented order, write a fresh durable local restore guard/journal, and retain private verified recovery copies of the current files/integrations/state. Pause owned schedules while committing. Stop only an affected/superseded owned connector through the bounded controller; abort if it cannot stop safely. Respect the shared e/Ctrl+C save-point contract; no automatic user backup archive is created.
5. Restore/migrate core data privately through JFFS transaction and inventoried tunnel data on verified Entware; rebuild only saved configured/prepared maintenance setup via current normal installers/templates and checked ABI binary acquisition. Never restore code/binary versions. Recoverable cross-filesystem stages preserve mode/On-Off/maintenance/preferences/drafts; transient token handoff derives only when needed.
6. Rebuild exact owned operational markers/jobs from current templates and activation table: native operational integration only for configured On; Cloudflared runtime/watchdog only for valid On; updater only for installed+maintenance_enabled+saved auto-update preference. Explicit Off has no service hooks/jobs despite reconstructed binary; owned rotation fragments are separately governed by logging preference/readiness in step 7. Remove stale owned operational integration without deleting saved data; preserve foreign control flow/order/concurrent edits and Scribe globals.
7. Restore eligible custom history only to mounted Entware; requested installed logging rebuilds current owned rules with usable Scribe/storage even for configured service Off, leaving its retained history rotating under Scribe independently. Runtime On/readiness gates file output. Preserve explicit logging uninstall/opt-out: no rule recreation/self-heal/enable from history presence. Deferred native-only history remains archived/reported; unavailable prerequisites keep output inactive/native work available. Never-configured features receive no logging setup.
8. Validate remote references read-only where needed for eligible On/current bootstrap; no Disabled API calls merely due restored files. Definitive auth rejection invokes fault/public Off+minimal failed-credential repair; identity/deleted-resource mismatch goes reviewed setup/import, no remote recreation. Throttle/transient wait follows shared policy, never token prompt/guessed Off.
9. Commit coherent data/runtime-authority generation then clear restore guard; resume eligible On/maintenance and fresh resolver/provider bootstrap, never historical success/queue/auth intent replay. No fabricated unconfigured setup or credentials/UUID. New auth fault is current local state distinct from advisory archive metadata.
10. Actual local artifact/generation/commit failure rolls back prior valid transaction or stays quarantined. Confirmed provider auth-Off/waiting outcome does not blindly rollback into prior faulty On or erase its durable fault; preserve restored locally valid data and route explicit repair. Report exact stage/outcome, preserve recovery/selected archive; no remote creation/deletion.

## Install, migration, repair, and uninstall

| Operation | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| First base install | Check retained identity/config/data/guard before defaults. Truly fresh install is passive native core/defaults all false/off, developer=false; valid completed retained removal instead rebuilds saved setup under reconstruction contract below. No provider recreation or wizard. |
| Dedicated DDNS setup | Run only from DDNS setup/import; validate/save DNS settings/credentials and stage its own hooks/jobs. Apply its fixed logfile policy on first fresh setup with ready Scribe; otherwise custom logging stays unavailable/off while native messages work. Reveal its re-detect after setup. Preserve Cloudflared/IP-Sync state. |
| Dedicated Cloudflared setup/install | A/B both use the common Entware binary/updater/watchdog installer at reviewed Save. A saves the dashboard token; B asks import `[y/N]`, where y prepares placement/re-detection/validation and n/Enter runs full named-tunnel creation with selected-file overwrite warnings. No application/DNS/policy creation. Activate one validated method only after reviewed Save/enable; apply size logging defaults on completed setup with ready Scribe, preserving saved policy/off choices. |
| Standalone Cloudflared maintenance install/reinstall | Choice 2 force-downloads checked official binary/current owned helpers; maintenance_enabled=true, preserves tunnel configured/enabled/data/logging. No token wizard/YAML/Scribe/provider setup. Watchdog/start only for valid On; binary transaction may stop/restart only currently On verified owned connector, helper repair alone leaves healthy process intact. |
| Cloudflared binary/updater/watchdog uninstall | Choice 2 stops verified owned connector, sets enabled=false/maintenance_enabled=false, detaches all owned integrations and removes verified owned/adopted recorded binary/helpers/old/staging on mounted Entware; installed=false only after cleanup. Keep configured tunnel data/preferences/logs. Unlike full manager KEEP removal, this explicitly changes tunnel activation to Off. Later On reinstalls missing binary/helpers without setup. |
| Dedicated IP-Sync setup | Run only from IP-Sync setup/import; validate/save account/list/credentials, reference the shared IPv4/IPv6 resolver policy and reconcile eligible shared discovery plus own event/retry integration. Prefer enabled DDNS observations; standalone runs shared resolution/list work only. Apply fixed logfile policy on first fresh setup with ready Scribe, otherwise native messages remain available. Reveal re-detect after setup. Do not install cloudflared, set up DDNS, duplicate source settings or reconfigure Scribe globally. |
| Reinstall / manager update | Explicit `u` followed by yes, or `uf`/reinstall, uses the same verified manager-only package transaction plus bounded comparison of trusted master authority, owned YAML trio and full derived JSON. Healthy matching state is a no-op with no runtime repair/resnapshot/restart/download/provider call or activation change. Repair validated owned drift only; preserve all user YAML/ingress changes. Defaults only for genuinely new config. No automatic startup check/install, feature wizard/binary install, healthy-service restart, hook takeover, or provider mutation. Optional future amtm trigger must use this same preservation contract. |
| Legacy detection | Identify the supplied scripts, tunnel files, old cron commands, logfile, and any matching rotation policy. Never execute/source a legacy secret-bearing script just to extract its settings. |
| Migration recovery | Preserve existing Cloudflare settings, credentials, affected hook originals, cron definitions, and relevant rotation policies in private transaction staging before modifying them. Preserve owned logs during moves. A persistent `CFBackup` archive is an optional explicit manual action. |
| Migration activation | Validate imported settings, determine tunnel management mode, stage new paths, preserve routing/certificates, switch hooks/jobs once, and use one deliberate lifecycle transition. Retain rollback material. |
| Legacy takeover | Remove only recognized legacy CF behavior and owned jobs. If unrelated DDNS logic cannot be separated safely, stop automatic takeover and prepare a reviewed integration instead of running duplicate providers. |
| Repair | Revalidate config/schema/permissions/guard and affected storage only: core settings/recovery need JFFS/RAM; operational tools follow R36/D55 and tunnel/log data has its own verified-storage scope. Recover interrupted transactions; actual damage contains owned scope and prompts reviewed repair. Native On repairs owned blocks/jobs; Off removes them without clearing values. Preserve foreign/concurrent edits; no remote-policy change or global Scribe defaults. |
| Full uninstall | `ao` reviewed scope/confirmation then exact `Wipe everything? [y/N]`; n/Enter retains primary config/data/intent, y adds owned data wipe. Shared guarded cleanup detaches hooks/jobs and removes package only after safe cleanup. Exit UI on completion. |
| Initialise / reset everything | `ao` explicit destructive scope/affirmative confirmation; reuse full-wipe cleanup while retaining current verified CFMgr package. Fresh passive defaults only after complete cleanup; no feature reinstall/provider/backup deletion. |
| Releases | Publish reviewed stable manager packages through `main`/versioned releases. Development builds are an explicit choice. Installation documentation is added only after a real install path has passed validation. |

A single public command may use packaged private helpers internally. The manager owns one navigation/configuration system with three dedicated feature setup flows; users do not manage separate scripts. Keep the layout small and preserve other features during individual setup/import/repair. CFMgr checks/installs its selected Entware packages under R36/D55; it does not format storage, install Entware itself or add router Python/venv/picker dependencies.

### Full uninstall, retained installation and passive reset

| Action / boundary | Required result |
| --- | --- |
| Shared confirmed cleanup | Review exact owned scope, then write durable installation/uninstall/reset intent BEFORE stops/hook deletion. Independent guard suppresses every affected background handler/rebuild while old config still says On. Use shared locks/generation/signal/power-loss containment, verified owned process bounded stop and exact hook/job removal. No blanket process-name kill. |
| Common removal scope | Detach ALL owned native/feature/maintenance hook blocks and jobs; remove verified Cloudflared binary/helpers/old/staging and owned logging integration/files/rotations. Full uninstall removes manager after safe reachable cleanup; reset retains package. KEEP preserves primary Entware config.yml/JSON/cert, JFFS master/cert copy, activation/preferences/retained marker and inventoried CFMgr-owned config.yml.bak. Foreign files/add-ons/hooks/processes, Entware/Scribe globals/native messages/manual archives/all remote resources survive. Before an actual overwrite/deletion of primary data, validate coherent master authority and trusted generation; missing/read-only storage or invalid/insufficient recovery data defers/refuses that destructive operation. |
| Full uninstall n / Enter (KEEP default) | Keep master full credentials/config/refs, primary Entware config.yml/JSON/cert, JFFS cert copy, inventoried CFMgr-owned config.yml.bak, activation/preferences and retained marker under non-runnable installation guard. Detach owned hooks/services/cron jobs; remove Cloudflared/watchdog/updater components and owned logging integrations/logs. KEEP does not delete primary tunnel files or `.bak`. Do not rewrite saved On just to stop. First INSTALL validates/reuses matching runtime files unchanged and derives only missing/actual owned drift from JFFS authority/cert copy using current templates/installers, without login/create; preserve ingress/saved state; Off stays Off. |
| Full uninstall y (WIPE) | After safe common unhook/stop/package/log cleanup, delete only owned JFFS master/config/cert copy and Entware config.yml/JSON/cert/.bak/tunnel data, state/ownership/recovery remnants. Same wipe routine for reset retaining manager. Foreign-root symlinks/shared-directory/other files survive; unresolved bounded cleanup metadata retained until complete. Backups/remote resources survive. |
| First INSTALL after completed KEEP removal | Detect retained marker/config/data before defaults and validate/migrate supported saved data. On expected verified mounted Entware, reuse matching valid primary YAML/JSON/cert unchanged; repair only missing/actual owned drift from JFFS authority/cert copy. Rebuild current trusted helpers and missing verified Cloudflared binary only for configured or prepared maintenance scope, even configured Off, without activating Off. Preserve full ingress/saved state; missing whole YAML requires full known-good recovery/import, never guessed starter. No wizard/browser login/remote create/activation from file presence. Missing Entware defers Cloudflared while native features remain available. Restore hooks from saved activation and log policy independently. Ordinary live u/uf never invokes this reconstruction path. |
| Reconstruction without Entware / invalid retained data | Defer dependent reconstruction under mount/tool gates, retaining saved intent and native recovery/status. DDNS/IP-Sync resumes only after shared prerequisites pass. Corrupt/missing required master holds/quarantines for repair, never fabricate starter YAML or lose ingress. Matching retained primary files may continue unchanged when valid; never rewrite them from stale authority. An incomplete uninstall/reset guard prohibits automatic reconstruction even when old enabled flags remain. |
| ao Initialise / reset everything | Explain irreversible owned current-data/log/integration deletion, require affirmative final confirmation; e/cancel/Enter before confirmed Save mutates nothing (empty input never wipes). Run the same destructive full-wipe routine retaining current verified entry/package, then initialize all unconfigured/disabled/maintenance false and developer=false. No feature reinstall or remote/manual-backup deletion. Deliberate completed reset is distinguishable from invalid retained setup. |
| Mount loss/read-only/storage uncertainty | Verify actual mount/path ownership before each binary/log/tunnel deletion and recheck commit. Missing/read-only drive refuses/defers affected cleanup; never stale `/opt` writes, premature package removal, fresh defaults or claim of full wipe. Preserve bounded cleanup guard/recovery metadata; interrupted cleanup cannot auto-resurrect or rebuild. Resume reviewed cleanup/repair until complete, then commit terminal installation state. |

The retained-install guard controls whether setup may execute; saved feature intent is data. Full KEEP uninstall preserves intent for later explicit installation, whereas Cloudflared maintenance uninstall deliberately saves Off. Exact installation_state/marker schema, ownership inventory, cleanup bounds and cross-filesystem recovery remain P1/P2/P5/P8 evidence work.

### Phase overview

| Phase | Status | Deliverable / exit condition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| P0 — Reference and plan | Done | Current source/config shape, hooks, schedules, logging gaps, storage layout, backup/restore requirements, and handoff captured here. |
| P1 — Contracts and evidence | In progress | Existing native/ABI/ownership/install/restore/logging evidence plus confirmed desired-state/schema/generation, provider auth-versus-throttle classifier/source-tested bounded runtime evidence, header/clock/scope/due dispatch, typed credential refs/fault repair intent and lossless owned YAML trio/JSON/certificate recovery. Policies selected, proof Pending. |
| P2 — Manager foundation | In progress: tested native foundations and partial health report | Native JFFS base install/update/config/recovery, mixed-key menus/status/configured-only quick CLI, manual developer flag, explicit Save/signals, integrity/install/removal guards, dependency gates, ownership and sanitized fixtures. |
| P3 — Tunnel lifecycle and updater | Pending | Existing bounded lifecycle/readiness/rollback/acquisition plus Scribe-independent rejection evidence, public auth-Off versus throttle On, scoped derived YAML-trio/JSON recovery and one eligible internal restart. No release blacklist/pin/cooldown/skip-version controls. |
| P4 — DDNS and Access | Pending | Native current-family resolver, authoritative add/remove/empty cleanup and DNS configured-definition recreation; fresh remote comparison/no-op, automatic drift correction and guided list setup. Current-state queues, bounded readback/cooldown/auth/completion. IPv6 provisional; POSIX shell with R36/D55 shared Entware prerequisites, no router Python. |
| P5 — Hooks and feature setup | Pending | Existing dedicated setup/OnOff/maintenance/foreign-preserving hooks and guarded uninstall/reconstruction/reset plus fx/default-yes launch fault prompt, serial typed credential-only repair/intent preservation, retained B management credentials and unknown-create-safe resumable setup. |
| P6 — Logging and Scribe integration | Pending | Three owned files/rules, Cloudflared tuning/fixed native policies, first-ready-setup defaults and scoped checks/re-detect/explicit Install-enable/Uninstall logging. Independent Scribe rotation survives service Off; absent-Scribe writer cleanup/owned deletion/mount deferral and opt-out preservation; native diagnostics without daemon syslog mirroring. |
| P7 — Backup and restore | Pending | Native mount/archive menus, router-local data-only `.tar.gz` in root CFBackup, 3–5 retention, current-code supported-schema migration/reconstruction with fresh guard and activation-aware Off-unhooked restore; no executable archive restore/downgrade. Failure/rollback validation, no backup schedule/initial fzf. |
| P8 — Router validation and release | Pending | Automated/BusyBox and authorized router evidence for declared support scope, complete docs/screenshots/model declaration/maintenance instructions and reviewed release. R34 permits live IPv6 gaps labelled untested/partially tested; never hide safety/test failures or imply full IPv6 validation. |

- [ ] P8.3 Document/verify optional amtm Personal scripts setup for `/jffs/scripts/cfmgr`, preserving chosen slots and explaining that launcher support does not provide amtm installer/updater membership.
- [ ] P8.4 Run complete automated/BusyBox checks for declared scope, record versions/results and authorized router evidence; verify docs/screenshots before stable promotion. R34/P8.6 permits absent full live IPv6 coverage with provisional labels. Never hide actual required failures using skips/xfail or weakened safety expectations.
- [ ] P8.5 Once implementation and required validation for declared support scope are complete (including honest R34 IPv6 qualifications), review the standing maintenance guidance in `AGENTS.md` against the actual code and developer guide. Cover module boundaries, supported runtime/dependencies, real test commands, config/schema migrations, update/release workflow, and authorization/secrets boundaries where useful. Keep phase IDs, current/next tasks, progress, session history and handoff state solely in this plan; do not add a P8.5 reminder to AGENTS.md. Link authoritative docs rather than duplicating the plan or assuming unimplemented behavior; preserve the file's existing local/ignored policy unless the user chooses to share it.
- [ ] P8.6 Record IPv6 automated/native results, any authorized live IPv6-only/dual-stack/DNS/list/policy checks and exact Not run cases separately. Full live IPv6 testing optional; document untested/partially tested status and router-/128-versus-LAN-client limitation. List readback never proves effective Access policy authorization; IPv4 passes never imply IPv6 proof.

## Development tests and documentation

Use Python with **pytest** on developer machines/CI to exercise the actual POSIX shell implementation. Python, its virtualenv and test dependencies are development tooling; do not deploy them with CFMgr or reintroduce a Python/venv requirement for DDNS/IP-Sync. Pytest's [fixture/testing guide](https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/getting-started.html) and Python's [venv documentation](https://docs.python.org/3/library/venv.html) support the selected workflow. Pin compatible Python/pytest requirements during P1; pytest plus the Python standard library is the initial harness, with additional test packages only for a demonstrated need.

| Planned development artifact | Purpose / contract |
| --- | --- |
| `requirements-dev.txt` | Declared reproducible pytest/test dependencies with compatible version bounds selected in P1; install inside the local virtualenv. No runtime requirements or global pip installs. |
| `.venv/` | Local ignored test environment; create with `python3 -m venv .venv`, recreate from requirements, never commit/package it. Existing ignore coverage already excludes it. |
| `tests/` and `tests/fixtures/` | Python tests/subprocess harness, fake router tree/tool responses/mounts/clock/provider payloads, and fully sanitized fixtures. Map tests to relevant requirements and V cases; keep helpers readable/reusable and assert observable contracts rather than copying implementation. |
| `pytest.ini` | Central discovery/registered markers such as unit, integration and busybox; bounded invocation and explicit reporting of unavailable environments. Exact options/marker definitions selected with harness implementation. |
| `docs/development.md` | Supported Python/host/BusyBox versions, venv/setup commands, requirements, focused/full test commands, fixture architecture, failure/concurrency reproductions, compatibility versus router-proof boundaries and contribution workflow. Link from root README when created and maintain as tests evolve. |

Proposed quick-start commands, to become runnable when those files/tests exist:

```sh
python3 -m venv .venv
. .venv/bin/activate
python -m pip install -r requirements-dev.txt
python -m pytest
python -m pytest -k ddns
python -m compileall tests
```

| Test level / coverage | Required evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Configuration and native API behavior | Parser/serializer escaped/nested/malformed/truncated/bounded JSON, public IPv4/IPv6/source inputs, credential redaction and curl invocation, DNS pagination/partial completion, IP-Sync identity/type/unchanged/write/readback and newest queue/expiry. Use Python JSON/IP reference behavior as an independent oracle for relevant native cases. |
| Menus, state and hook integration | Drive isolated shell subprocesses with menu inputs/signals/fake firmware tools; test setup/defaults, A/B import/fresh/overwrite, On/Off retention, exact markers/foreign content/concurrent edits, scope-specific cron/mount gates, status and absent Entware/Scribe behavior. Assert before/after files/jobs/processes, exit status and notices. |
| Lifecycle, timing and interrupted transactions | Deterministic mock clock/fault injection for NTP/ping/DNS wait, growing/capped retry/full reset, overlap/locks/manual stop, binary/manager update rollback, e/Ctrl+C/save points, corrupt journal/config and unknown remote-create outcome. Test interruptions at meaningful boundaries and prove owned cleanup preserves unrelated work. |
| Logging and archive/restore | Scribe detection/on-off/reinstall healing, fixed versus tunable rules, concurrent appends/rotation, safe manifest/extraction/path rejection, source/destination mount loss/full disk, retention/naming, native-only/mixed payload restore and optional history deferral. Host fakes do not prove installed logrotate or router mount behavior. |
| POSIX/BusyBox compatibility | Shell syntax checks plus actual BusyBox shell/applet execution on a supported development/CI environment. macOS/default host shell success alone is insufficient. Missing BusyBox is reported explicitly; required compatibility tests must pass before claiming router support. Tool/image/version setup belongs in the developer guide. |
| Authorized router acceptance | Reboot/real Entware timing, native tools/CA/SHA256, direct asset ABI/provenance/foreign-controller ownership, Scribe/logrotate, safe provider checks and actual data-only restore/uninstall/retained reconstruction/reset remain separately authorized P8 work. Host passes cannot satisfy live evidence requirements. |

Default automated runs use isolated temporary trees, fake tools/providers and sanitized credentials; no real router paths, credentials, provider writes or live-service actions. Subprocess timeouts/owned-child cleanup prevent hung tests; control race ordering instead of relying on long sleeps. Record pass/fail/skip and shell/tool versions, tie failures/fixes to matrix IDs, and update tests alongside each meaningful implementation package. Explain existing-test expectation changes before editing them and preserve the verified contract. CI uses the same declared environment/full suite; no mandatory coverage percentage or Python-harness coverage metric can substitute for meaningful shell behavior and failure-path evidence.

IPv6 fixture/native compatibility evidence is distinct from live acceptance. Missing optional live coverage keeps the provisional label and cannot justify weakening a failing automated safety assertion. Guided IP-Sync setup documentation must cover token/account permissions, numbered versus exact-name selection, optional quote handling, second-list creation/Refresh and Access policy references.

Build user documentation as implementation lands, with root README linking the current guides and the developer guide. Cover shared prerequisites and scoped tunnel/logging additions, install/update/uninstall/migration, DDNS/IP-Sync tokens/settings, Cloudflared A/B creation/import/switching, logging/redetect/healing, status/outages, manual backup/restore and recovery. Store useful screenshots under `.images/`, reference them with relative links from `docs/`, and refresh when tested workflows change. Cloudflare dashboard captures must hide tokens/API keys/account/tunnel IDs, private domains/IPs and unnecessary account details. Use placeholders/sanitized examples and label incomplete/unverified flows; do not publish runnable install/setup claims before the corresponding implementation has passed validation.

The user guide must explain Cloudflare account creation and a domain with Cloudflare-managed DNS for DDNS/public tunnel hostnames, including domains registered elsewhere. These are documentation instructions, not added script prompts or a changed setup flow. `docs/setup.md` contains the draft walkthrough and screenshot placeholders; README links it briefly. Keep drafts current during implementation and fully polish README, user/developer guides and the dedicated architecture document at the end.

## Validation matrix

All runtime results start as pending. Record IPv6 automated/live subcases separately: optional unrun live cases are Not run with provisional R34 labels, actual safety failures remain failures. Use sanitized fixtures/mocks first; source checks, local simulations, and live router/Cloudflare proof are recorded separately. Do not claim connectivity from PID/process-survival checks. Existing test changes should be explained before changing their expectations.

| Test ID | Scenario | Required result | Evidence status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| V01 | Entware absent/unmounted/read-only/wrong volume/lost mid-operation; mixed native and tunnel features | Base install/update/DDNS/IP-Sync/config/status/recovery remain usable from firmware/JFFS/RAM. Only Cloudflared/binary/custom-log work defers; eligible cron continues firing, no stale `/opt` writes or native-feature failure caused solely by missing logs/storage. | Pending |
| V02 | Supported/unsupported architecture and native tools/JSON capabilities | Supported paths work with firmware tools and permitted minimal dependencies; no router Python/venv/picker installation; missing essential capabilities fail before activation. | Pending |
| V03 | Boot/WAN/watchdog/manual starts overlap | One owned connector; preserved other hooks; no blocking boot/network loop. | Pending |
| V04 | Time/DNS/IPv4 readiness failure and recovery | Deferred state, bounded checks, restart does not stop a healthy process before readiness, and recovery on a later trigger. | Pending |
| V05 | Update metadata/download/version/backup/stop failure | Old binary/service protected; failure is bounded and recoverable. | Pending |
| V06 | Interruption before/after binary or manager activation | Compatible package/known-good binary restored; pending markers and backups protected. | Pending |
| V07 | IPv4-only/dual-stack/IPv6-only, family disable/return, partial DNS update/delete/create and source failures | Current active values maintained, confirmed inactive managed A/AAAA removed and configured definitions recreated on return. All desired writes/removals verified before full owned callback success; unknown source defers affected scope. Valid source states still feed IP-Sync. IPv6-only live proof optional under R34. | Pending |

Validated original cert-byte JFFS copy recovers matching runtime cert; seed only from trusted validated cert, never arbitrary/foreign cert. Copying cannot renew rejected auth. Backup both cert copies with full YAML coherently. KEEP preserves primary and recovery files including owned .bak; WIPE/reset remove owned data, foreign files survive. | Pending |
| V55 | NEW create interrupted/lost result/JSON or JFFS write failure, CLI rollback, mount disappears at each child stage | Small durable intent/private fresh output before child; complete confirmed UUID+credentials can recover same local generation, no second create. ID-only/unconfirmed/failed/incomplete creation prompts manual dashboard cleanup, no automatic listing/adoption/lookup/recreate. Nonzero/CLI rollback does not prove remote absence; no activation of known deleted resource. Mount loss cancels/reaps owned child boundedly, reports unavailable drive/possible failed tunnel, returns menu with recovery/old generation preserved; native/status/Off still work. Same verified returning mount never resumes failed creation. | Pending |
| V56 | Missing JSON regenerated from wrong master secret; existing-ID fetch/cert re-login, unsupported/deleted/wrong account/permission and explicit replacement | Regeneration never proves auth or clears fault: authoritative rejection Off/unhook/fx. Supported fetch saves matching account/UUID/new secret via private stage, no create/ID change/token leakage/login-only success; refusal/network/throttle/storage cannot silently change mode/ID or force recreation. Conclusively unrecoverable offers default-no reviewed replacement/manual old-tunnel deletion; e/decline Off. Preserve FULL validated ingress/options/comments and owned .bak before owned-trio change; malformed YAML retained for manual re-patch, never silently discard rules. Backup optional .bak validation/inert restore. KEEP preserves `.bak`; WIPE/reset also remove owned `.bak` with the other owned data, while foreign files survive. | Pending |
| V57 | Healthy/mismatched manager u/uf/reinstall, first install after KEEP, actual Off, auth_fault, corrupt master/nonowned YAML, missing Entware, user ingress edit, cert copy provenance/size/cap failure, WIPE/reset | Every retained-config manager reinstall/update uses the selected-method gate before activation: B checks JSON account/UUID/secret and owned YAML trio against validated master; A checks saved token/identity/private handoff without requiring inactive B files. Unconfigured/native-only state adds no tunnel requirements. Matching files cause no forced rewrite/restart/download/provider call/activation change; validated owned drift is repaired under guard, only a real runtime change can restart eligible On. Local regeneration does not clear auth_fault or count as remote auth. Missing mount defers dependent reconciliation/activation while native recovery/status remains available; operational work enforces R36/D55. Corrupt authority or invalid user YAML is preserved/quarantined. KEEP preserves primary Entware YAML/JSON/cert and owned .bak plus JFFS master/cert copy; first install uses current code without wizard/login/create and retains ingress/Off. Cert recovery accepts original bytes only from trusted provenance/account and format; same valid generation causes no redundant JFFS write, cap/space failure is reported. WIPE/reset delete only owned data across both roots; foreign files/backups survive. | Pending |
| V58 | Complete source-to-port map, Python absent/poisoned, dry run On/Off/auth rejection/cooldown, descriptions and expired/newer queue | Runtime package/call paths contain no Python/venv/reference-helper fallback; DNS enumeration, IP-Sync and updater metadata run natively. Every baseline has an equivalent or documented improvement. Target check GETs only, never queues/publishes/enables or writes success cache/history; shared cooldown/auth containment still applies, Off stays Off and failed/unverified checks never report would-replace. Metadata and frozen queue-age/batch limits retained. | Pending |
| V59 | Worker killed while curl survives; connector/detached child inherits descriptors; stale lock path; PID reuse/foreign connector | In-flight provider write remains serialized until child exit/quiescence; newest work survives. Long-lived connector retains no controller/update lock. No live-lock unlink/recreate, process-name kill or signal to a reused/foreign PID; child timeouts and lock order remain bounded without holding queue/config locks through network/user waits. | Pending |
| V60 | Idle/On acquisition; older/equal/newer version; crash at backup/stop/marker/rename/start/clear; outage or Off races | Binary downgrade refused before download and after locked recheck; force does not bypass it, protected rollback is permitted. Idle Off/unconfigured repeated install/recovery never requires runtime credentials or starts a connector; no permanent startup-required marker. On pending state survives inconclusive PID/outage, valid backup recovers missing/bad primary, unsafe paths fail closed, and rollback success reports the attempted update failed. | Pending |
| V61 | Hostile curlrc/environment/redirects, overflow/truncation, DNS pagination/type errors, family loss, overlap/cooldown | Native bounded complete validation; lookup failure never authorizes deletion or creation. Confirmed family disable/absence deletes managed records, current-family return recreates configured definitions. Unsupported/unknown evidence and partial writes/removals not success. No stale/duplicate callback, token output or cooldown bypass. | Pending |
| V62 | Cloudflare manual address/extra-entry/name/managed-metadata edits or managed DNS deletion with unchanged local IP | Next eligible scheduled/event/manual reconciliation reads and automatically restores current local desired state without conflict review. Missing configured DNS definitions can be recreated after complete enumeration; a missing whole list cannot be guessed/recreated. Bounded retries handle external churn and eventual readback, never promise continuous atomic remote equality. | Pending |
| V63 | Wizard versus manual config edits, missing revision bump, later Off/auth guard, write failure and mixed package generation | Last accepted wizard Save overwrites its explicit field set over concurrent manual edits without conflict prompt, preserves unrelated fields and real guards, and publishes a new generation. Later valid changes/Off win; workers compare content identity as well as generation. Malformed authority requires recovery. Compatible package per invocation and truthful guard-persistence limits. | Pending |
| V64 | Event bursts/settle, long record sets/readiness probes, shutdown/unmount/watchdog race, EOF/no TTY/HUP/masked input | Whole-action budgets and early-event debounce enforced; hooks return within verified firmware bounds, delayed workers recheck generation/On. Shutdown suppression prevents respawn without erasing saved intent. EOF/no TTY never accepts default yes or loops; signals restore echo and safely cancel/recover owned work. | Pending |

Old active/clear queues never replay after family state changes. | Pending |

Draft never overwrites active/shared refs or enables incomplete setup; resume missing/invalid stage. | V72 | Empty IP-list clearing, inactive A/AAAA deletion, family return, lost PATCH/DELETE/POST response and offline cleanup | Preserve list ID/name/policy reference; clear using documented value removal and verify complete empty state, never assume PUT [] works.
