Delete only configured DNS scope; retain logical definitions/attributes, re-enumerate lost responses and recreate active missing records without duplicate POST/stale-ID assumptions. No transport leaves pending cleanup, no fabricated remote success. Native tools only; verify both writes and removals. | Pending |
| V73 | All-feature boot/outage safety: absent/late mount, unsynced NTP, dead network/DNS, tunnel exits or disconnects while PID lives, prerequisites returning in different orders, busy locks/hung children, inherited output pipes, event/callback storms, low RAM/full JFFS and corrupt recovery/config/early crashes | Boot/foreign hooks meet P1.19 deadlines while affected work waits; no startup/publication/network update before all scoped gates pass. One eligible recovery follows readiness, preserves On/current desired state and verifies connectivity separately from PID. Thin hooks/direct handlers share common gates; missing helpers cannot open menus or block foreign work. Publishing requires Internet/NTP and the shared Entware prerequisites; optional file logging waits independently. Pipes close despite live children; admission/resources/deadlines/cleanup/backoff stay bounded. Permanent faults require repair/change; brief survival cannot reset crash history. Teardown prevents late respawn; zero reboot/global service restart/recursive hooks. Isolated fixtures plus separately authorized router evidence; source/syntax alone cannot pass. | Pending |
| V74 | Dependency bootstrap/install/update/reinstall, missing/broken tools, wrong ABI/libraries, unavailable feeds, failed opkg, interrupted installs, busy locks, mount loss, event storms and explicit force reinstall | No operational startup before all selected required packages/capabilities pass. Bounded serialized repair and next-launch recheck/retry; native diagnostics remain available. SHA package required with separate OpenSSL bootstrap/edge fallback. Force reinstall targets CFMgr's selected Entware package set only, preserves config/On-Off, performs post-probes and never runs Cloudflared daemon/hook/worker reinstall or unrelated upgrades. Use installed opkg for package/library resolution and configured repositories; never manually bootstrap dependency IPKs. | V75 | Health-check aliases, context fields, missing dependencies, unavailable Entware, unusable tools/ABI/libraries, unsupported arguments, upstream prerequisite failures, unsafe diagnostics and interrupted probes | --diagnostic and --doctor produce identical results without normal startup/install/repair. Required/scoped packages explicitly inventoried; missing storage is not missing-package proof. Whitelisted sanitized model/firmware/kernel/ABI/version fields only; no secrets/identifiers/raw dumps. Synthetic probes clean only their private temporary data; no service/config/provider/package mutation or network. | Pending |

Evidence collection stays private and read-only until a deployment or provider mutation is authorized. Do not commit captured tokens, tunnel IDs, hostnames, certificates, raw config dumps, or real log archives. Sanitized operating examples belong in `docs/`.

### Initial implementation evidence — 2026-10-08

These are observations and selected constraints, not a completed P1 or supported-router declaration. Raw terminal captures and downloaded source remain in ignored `.tmp/`.

| Evidence | Confirmed result / implication |
| --- | --- |
| Live target | User-confirmed and banner/NVRAM-verified GT-AX11000 `3004.388.12_2`; Linux `4.1.51`, kernel `aarch64`, BusyBox `1.25.1`. Native BusyBox/curl ELF headers are ARM32; installed Cloudflared is AArch64. Kernel architecture alone is not a userspace ABI/arithmetic check. |
| Native arithmetic | `/bin/sh` gives `-2147483648` for `2147483647 + 1` and `1 << 31`. Design shell arithmetic for signed 32-bit bounds; never build unsigned IPv4 integers or unchecked timestamp/size products with host-width assumptions. Pinned 378.50/380.70/386.14 source samples also indicate signed 32-bit arithmetic, with no sampled lower limit; see [compatibility evidence](docs/compatibility.md) for TLS, utility, kernel and 2038 qualifications. |
| Native tools | Native `awk`, `flock -n`, `hexdump`, `readlink -f`, tar/gzip, ping deadlines, OpenSSL and `/proc/uptime` are available. `command -v` returns 127; `timeout`, `mktemp`, `stat`, `sha256sum`, `base64`, `od`, `setsid` and `dig` are absent from the isolated firmware PATH. `nslookup` exposes only host/server lookup, so native SRV readiness still needs a proved solution. |
| Transport / integrity | Firmware `/usr/sbin/curl` is `8.17.0`, native OpenSSL `1.1.1w`; native CA bundle is readable. Read-only option inspection confirms connect/total timeouts, protocol controls, interface selection and response limits. OpenSSL SHA-256 and base64 decode work on synthetic stdin. HTTPS, streamed-overflow, proxy-isolation and provider behavior are still untested. |
| Storage / dates | JFFS is jffs2, RAM tmpfs; current resolved Entware resides on mounted ext4. Mode/mount/free-space reads are evidence only, not write/atomicity/power-loss proof. C-locale `%x` yields month/day/two-digit-year despite the router timezone; do not infer the desired regional archive order. |
| Current logging | Scribe `v3.2.12`, included `/opt/etc/logrotate.d`, syslog-ng fragment include and `scribeLogRotate` at `5 0 * * *` observed. Existing Cloudflared rule uses `size 5M`, `rotate 0`, `copytruncate`, `missingok`, `notifempty`; this is migration evidence, not automatic adoption of new defaults. `/tmp/syslog.log` points into Entware, so no-Entware history availability cannot be assumed. No rotation/debug/service action run. |
| Existing schedules | Read-only inspection confirms watchdog every minute, binary update `37 3 * * *`, DDNS `0 0 * * *`, IP-Sync retry `*/5 * * * *`; no jobs modified. |
| Matched firmware source | Official tag `3004.388.12_2` resolves to commit `433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f`. `services-start`, `services-stop`, WAN and DHCP event hooks are async; custom DDNS, post-mount and unmount use 120-second outer waits. Those firmware alarms are not CFMgr budgets or process-tree cleanup. |
| DDNS completion policy | Matched `ddns_custom_updated_main` takes only result, with no request identity; missing argument means success. It writes current global DDNS/WAN state, so a detached old callback cannot be assumed safe. On resume, the user selected a short strictly capped DDNS wait to preserve Merlin success/failure reporting; other hooks remain prompt. Exact total deadline, overlap/current-invocation checks and callback failure handling still require implementation proof. No callback has been executed during evidence collection. |

Source anchors: [hook dispatch](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/shared/scripts.c#L54), [custom DDNS wait](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/rc/services.c#L5055), [global completion callback](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/rc/services.c#L21018).

Further pinned source review found a synchronous boot path through start_wan/start_wan_if/wan_up/start_ddns. services-start is launched before start_wan; success_start_service/start_service_ready precede later startup work. NTP synchronization can invoke DDNS too. None of those facts alone proves boot has released its caller. A matching custom service-event receipt with verified PID1 ancestry could establish that PID1 reached its post-startup notification loop, but this is an unimplemented candidate. [Boot ordering](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/rc/init.c#L21452) · [later flags](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/rc/init.c#L21866) · [service-event dispatch](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/rc/services.c#L13620).

The public GT-AX11000 notify_rc.o selected by the pinned build was disassembled, not executed; SHA256 8e86285c9d8167800ba78291786d76fe23f0a8be5fb91f8f2ba5db895fa4d1c4. It polls an occupied rc_service with up to 15 one-second sleeps, then skips if still busy or replaces the value and signals PID1. No visible atomic check-and-write/append protects concurrent foreign notifications; service_main also discards its return status. Do not inject a boot-barrier event or write raw rc_service on the assumption that delivery/concurrent preservation is proved. An actual event receipt would acknowledge delivery, but neither solves overlapping direct DDNS callers. This is public compiled-source evidence, not an installed-library match. [Object](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/shared/prebuild/GT-AX11000/notify_rc.o) · [service status handling](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/rc/services.c#L20802).

A read-only boot-release candidate observes PID1 entering its post-startup signal loop, then records a private boot-specific latch. The sole active sigwaitinfo call in the reviewed init.c is after synchronous startup, start_wan and check_services; earlier manufacturing branches use pause instead. The matching GT-AX11000 build specifies ARM userland on an AArch64 kernel, whose ARM compatibility rt_sigtimedwait number is 177. Read-only installed probes confirmed /proc/1/exe resolves to /sbin/rc, its ELF header is 32-bit little-endian ARM, three syscall observations were 177 and two wchan observations were do_sigtimedwait. A generic sleeping state is insufficient, and that syscall number alone does not identify its caller. Exclusion of earlier linked-library call sites remains unproved; do not enable a foreground DDNS wait from this candidate yet. Failure to establish boot release must defer promptly. [Post-startup wait](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/rc/init.c#L21944). No startup was exercised and no custom service event or NVRAM write was performed.

### Dependency amendment — selected during implementation

**Current precedence:** the 2026-10-09 dependency-route correction near the top of this plan is authoritative: use installed Entware opkg with normal dependency resolution. Earlier direct-IPK staging, restricted local-candidate/offline-root and `--nodeps` passages below record explored designs/source evidence; they are not the selected operational installation route. Reconcile them during the next session before implementing dependency installation.

The user permits a small documented set of Entware dependencies when native utilities would make implementation unreliable or unnecessarily complex. This supersedes the absolute native-only/no-jq restrictions in R04/R10/R11/R25/R27/R28/R35, the initial dependency table, D-series decisions and their associated P/V cases only to the extent that a selected required tool needs Entware. Preserve the original native-first shell design, minimal packages, JFFS/RAM authority, optional logging independence and storage safety. Python/venv remain development-only. CFMgr does not install Entware, silently switch feeds or upgrade the whole Entware installation.

1. Select exact capabilities and minimal packages from official architecture/kernel feeds; document native equivalents and alternatives. The shared set is jq, coreutils-timeout and coreutils-sha256sum. Use ordinary jq without regex functions, bind-dig only for tunnel DNS capability, and the flock package only when native flock is insufficient. Kernel architecture alone does not identify userspace ABI. Package availability, upstream kernel/library minimums and installed functional checks are separate evidence.
2. Before operational startup, re-check required executable paths, verified storage identity, supported behavior and usability; an installed package record or cached success is insufficient. A required missing/broken tool triggers a bounded installation attempt when the correct Entware mount/opkg/network prerequisites exist. No startup, publishing or dependent update work proceeds on failure.
3. The next launch checks again and may retry required installation; never store a permanent failure latch. Perform these checks during manager install/update/reinstall too. Concurrent launches coalesce behind one dependency worker/lock. Automatic events obey aggregate deadlines/backoff and cannot create package-install storms or block firmware hooks. No busy-loop reinstall within one launch.
4. Re-check the capability after successful opkg exit; reject an unusable executable, wrong ABI, unmet library requirement, failed probe or changed mount. A completed package command alone does not authorize startup. Preserve saved On/Off intent and report the actual failure without replacing valid configuration.
5. The latest SHA256 instruction makes coreutils-sha256sum a required installation package because it exists across the same feed baseline as jq. Prefer sha256sum for every verification; keep native OpenSSL for bootstrap and exceptional fallback, but do not call setup successful after failed required-package installation. Required-dependency retry is separate from missing-code repair: ordinary launches never fetch arbitrary missing CFMgr modules.
6. Recovery/status diagnostics and safe Off/removal must remain possible without starting feature work. Scoped dependencies gate their affected work; a genuinely shared required tool gates all operational startup. Hooks return promptly and delegate bounded package work only after safe admission; missing mount/network exits waiting rather than installing into a stale path.
7. The final README and compatibility/development guides must state the selected minimum kernels, highest tested/source-known kernel families, ABI limits and exact installed packages/transitive requirements. Do not invent a universal upper bound or call untested archive combinations supported. The goal is broad practical Merlin coverage, not acceptance inferred solely from an Entware feed name.
8. Advanced options provides Reinstall Entware dependencies. Force-reinstall CFMgr's selected direct package set (including active scoped additions), then verify every required capability; opkg resolves library dependencies normally. Handle an already usable alternate provider such as jq-full without blindly installing a conflicting jq package. Do not reinstall every package on the router, force unrelated base-library upgrades, reset config/activation, or perform Cloudflared daemon/hook/worker reinstallation. Retain bounded cancellation/recovery and a native recovery entry when the failed dependency prevents ordinary menus. No independent feature installation is implied.

Official feed research confirmed all three shared packages plus bind-dig/flock in aarch64-k3.10, armv7sf-k3.2, mipselsf-k3.4, withdrawn armv7sf-k2.6 and the old MIPSEL archive. See [the compatibility matrix](docs/compatibility.md#-entware-coverage-and-selected-packages) for exact sampled versions, transitive libraries and kernel/ABI evidence. Maintained feeds are the normal development target; ARM 2.6.36.4 and MIPS 2.6.22.19 remain separate unaccepted legacy profiles. Current official Merlin source includes 4.19.183/4.19.294 in addition to the live 4.1.51 sample; this is not a maximum support claim.

Bootstrap source review found native timeout/setsid/start-stop-daemon/job control absent. Opkg's per-download wget timeout is not a whole-command/maintainer-script deadline. The earlier P1.20 proof route, now unselected under the opkg correction, used a privately staged coreutils-timeout executable from the exact official architecture's HTTPS index/IPK, verified size/SHA256 with native OpenSSL and fixed-member stdout extraction only, before any opkg mutation. The staged executable must load and pass probes against the verified Entware base libraries; missing/broken base libraries fail closed. Bound download/expanded bytes and validate archive layout/member identity before executing any stage. Package builder uses a gzip-tar outer container; older archive compatibility still needs proof. Never assume arbitrary archive extraction or an unverified binary is safe. [Package format](https://github.com/Entware/Entware/blob/969c703e6fd8b2ad84d82affaeb14b48d1fcb105/scripts/ipkg-build#L182) · [opkg transport](https://github.com/openwrt/opkg-lede/blob/80503d94e356476250adaf1f669ee955ec26de76/libopkg/opkg_download.c#L155).

Use a CFMgr-owned stable native-flock lock for its own dependency attempts; opkg's lockf/fcntl lock is a different protocol, never stolen/unlinked or treated as interchangeable. Supervision timeout can leave partial package state; post-probes and later recheck/retry remain mandatory. Actual bootstrap extraction, command supervision, mount loss and opkg fault behavior are unimplemented/unverified.

An additional read-only SSH session confirmed installed jq 1.8.1-2, coreutils-timeout/coreutils-sha256sum 9.9-2, glibc/librt/libpthread 2.27-12 and libssp 8.4.0-12, architecture aarch64-3.10. Version queries load successfully and synthetic jq field selection/escaping works. These do not prove timeout group cleanup, package installation or complete JSON integration. jq output should explicitly disable terminal colors in machine paths. No dependency installation, feed update, CFMgr deployment or service mutation is authorized on the live router in this development session; the SSH session was closed after reads.

### Checksum backend clarification — selected during implementation

This policy incorporates the user's later requirement to install the matching Entware checksum package:

1. Every update verification tries a usable `sha256sum` first. Resolve approved native paths, or the verified mounted Entware command; never use an arbitrary inherited PATH or stale `/opt` location.
2. If `sha256sum` is unavailable, native `openssl dgst -sha256` is the fallback. Normalize exactly one 64-hex digest, check command/read status and compare with the trusted expected digest. Version validation remains a separate required check; neither backend bypasses it.
3. Include coreutils-sha256sum in the required shared installation set. Launch and manager install/update/reinstall check/repair it under R36/D55, and explicit Reinstall Entware dependencies includes it. Failed required installation blocks successful operational startup and can be retried next launch. OpenSSL remains bootstrap/edge fallback, not a waiver of installation failure. Do not install the whole coreutils suite.
4. The current installed package is `coreutils-sha256sum` `9.9-2`, providing `/opt/libexec/sha256sum-coreutils` and the `/opt/bin/sha256sum` alternatives symlink. Its declared dependencies are `libc`, `libssp`, `librt`, `libpthread`, and `coreutils`; the installed `coreutils` metadata package lists no files. Resolve package availability/dependencies on each supported feed, never hardcode this feed/version or guess from the symlink alone.
5. If no verified hashing backend works, fail before replacing code/data. Native recovery diagnostics remain usable; shared operational prerequisites are enforced separately. Never waive integrity because a package install or fallback failed.

The live read-only package query and synthetic-input digest comparison passed; no opkg update/install or router file/service mutation was performed. Runtime implementation and fault-path tests are pending.

### Storage identity and package containment — remaining proof

Matched firmware enables native blkid, readlink-follow and test predicates -O/-L/-f/-x/-b/-d. Direct `blkid DEVICE` reads the block device without writing but returns zero even for missing/unrecognized identity; require actual unambiguous UUID output. Modern -s/-o options are not supported, and this build omits TYPE output. Its display routine does not escape quoted labels: a label-only volume can manufacture a UUID-looking attribute without a real UUID. Consequently label-bearing output cannot establish identity, even if its grammar looks valid. Native parsing rejects that ambiguity; a separate source, such as bounded verified ext superblock bytes, remains to be proved for labelled volumes. Earlier router blkid reads establish observed output, not an injection-safe identity algorithm. Mountinfo supplies live mount/device/root/source/filesystem information, not a persistent UUID; mount IDs, labels and /dev/sd names alone do not prove the same volume across reboot. Bind-mount roots and the relative Entware path also matter. UUID is filesystem identity, not proof of unique hardware against cloned UUIDs. [blkid implementation](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/busybox/util-linux/blkid.c#L18) · [unescaped display](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/busybox/util-linux/volume_id/get_devname.c) · [native test](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/busybox/coreutils/test.c#L630).

The fifth closed read-only SSH session confirmed /opt is a relative link to tmp/opt; its resolved covering mount is block-backed ext4 with rw,nodev,relatime, and the direct-device query yields a 36-character UUID. Actual volume paths/UUID remain private. Native ls -ldn reports the Entware directory root-owned, mode 755; test -O/-L/-f and /opt/lib/opkg/status presence work. Native id is absent; whitelisted /proc/self/status Uid fields establish the process identity instead. These reads prove neither successful writes nor race-free mount-loss behavior.

Pinned opkg source does not offer a generic volume-anchoring option. A restricted P1.20 candidate keeps an inherited directory FD for the verified Entware target and uses a private RAM offline root containing only opt -> /proc/self/fd/N, with private config/temp/list paths. Core payload/status/metadata/lock/alternatives paths then follow the retained mount; canonical /opt file-list entries are preserved. A direct destination equal to the Entware FD would instead create nested opt paths. This candidate is **not yet selected as a proven installer**: offline mode skips maintainer scripts; forcing them restores hardcoded host-path access. A conffile failure-unwind path also unlinks canonical backup paths outside the offline root. Source research therefore requires an audited selected closure with no unsupported scripts or conffiles in either new or replaced packages before using this route. [Path construction](https://github.com/openwrt/opkg-lede/blob/80503d94e356476250adaf1f669ee955ec26de76/libopkg/opkg_conf.c#L81) · [script handling](https://github.com/openwrt/opkg-lede/blob/80503d94e356476250adaf1f669ee955ec26de76/libopkg/pkg.c#L1355) · [failure cleanup](https://github.com/openwrt/opkg-lede/blob/80503d94e356476250adaf1f669ee955ec26de76/libopkg/opkg_install.c#L753).

The coreutils commands and flock use alternatives metadata rather than payload links. ELF loaders and RUNPATH still reference /opt, so RAM extraction is not isolated execution. The restricted retained-FD/offline-root route remains incomplete after the execution audit below; it must not proceed using explicit loader flags alone. CFMgr must not repair/upgrade base libraries, run package scripts, install unaudited dependency closure or configure unrelated partial packages. Validate both incoming and replaced metadata, destination symlinks, inherited descriptors, actual free space on the retained volume, and dynamic-loader resolution before mutation is implemented. Software deadlines can contain ordinary owned processes; they cannot promise to kill/reap uninterruptible kernel storage IO by a deadline. Such unresolved attempts must prevent overlapping package workers.

**Execution audit, 2026-10-08:** Entware glibc 2.27 probes and reads `/opt/etc/ld.so.preload` before program startup even when directly invoked with `--library-path` and `--inhibit-cache`. Absolute dependency names, RUNPATH and `/opt/lib` defaults provide further fresh-path lookups. Therefore selecting an executable and loader through the retained descriptor does not contain execution. Verified dynamic `unshare` has the same loader boundary before its namespace syscall. Opkg also executes external `gzip` through `execlp`; loader command-line options do not carry into that child. Numeric tar ownership did not establish a mandatory NSS lookup in the restricted extraction path, but conditional NSS loading remains another path to account for. [Entware preload path](https://github.com/Entware/Entware/blob/969c703e6fd8b2ad84d82affaeb14b48d1fcb105/toolchain/glibc/patches/2.27/9999-ldconfig.patch#L34-L44) · [Loader startup](https://github.com/bminor/glibc/blob/23158b08a0908f381459f273a984c6fd328363cb/elf/rtld.c#L1606-L1700) · [gzip child](https://github.com/openwrt/opkg-lede/blob/80503d94e356476250adaf1f669ee955ec26de76/libbb/gzip.c#L91-L124).

The next candidate is a firmware-native isolated root that establishes the anchored `/opt` view before any Entware ELF starts. Matched BusyBox source supports `/bin/mount -n -i -o bind /proc/self/fd/9 TARGET` without fstab/helper lookup, followed by `/usr/sbin/chroot ROOT /absolute/program`. The kernel follows the retained descriptor and rejects an already-detached source at bind time; chroot changes root and current directory before executing the program. The ninth brief SSH session confirmed chroot/mount/umount/mknod executable paths and read help text plus `/dev/null` character-device metadata, then closed. It performed no mount, chroot, file creation or package execution. Propagation, helper/library closure, bounded ownership/cleanup and interruption behavior still require proof; chroot is not a security sandbox against hostile root code. [Native bind path](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/busybox/util-linux/mount.c#L1998-L2030) · [Kernel bind](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src-rt-5.02axhnd/kernel/linux-4.1/fs/namespace.c#L2149-L2181) · [Native chroot](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/busybox/coreutils/chroot.c#L33-L43).

Actual opkg executables are fully static ELF at startup: no interpreter or dynamic segment. This does not remove their runtime path/subprocess behavior. GNU gzip is dynamic, needs the selected Entware loader/libc and also libgcc on ARM/MIPS, and has `/opt/lib` RUNPATH. Each control archive contains only `control`, with no scripts/conffiles. A staged gzip ELF is a proposed confined helper, not a new installed prerequisite yet; its gunzip/zcat shell wrappers are unnecessary for opkg's direct `gzip -d -c` call. Exact artifacts and analysis are ignored under `.tmp/package-elf-audit/`; native source evidence is in `.tmp/native-chroot-research/`. No downloaded package was executed. Full library closure and actual lifecycle acceptance remain pending.

The matched kernel exposes the retained directory's mount instance through /proc/self/fdinfo/N mnt_id. Mainline introduced this in 3.15; package feed kernel names do not prove it, and older vendor kernels require a verified backport or separate alternative. Read-only native probes confirmed one decimal mnt_id record on an inherited descriptor opened at /opt, successful native test -ef and readlink against that descriptor. No volume path/UUID was published. Device/inode equality alone cannot distinguish bind mounts; an FD surviving lazy unmount must not count as attached storage without its matching namespace mount record. Never reopen readlink's displayed path as a substitute for retaining the FD. Actual acquisition/race/mount-loss behavior remains unimplemented. [Descriptor identity](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src-rt-5.02axhnd/kernel/linux-4.1/fs/proc/fd.c#L19) · [Linux 3.15](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v3.15/fs/proc/fd.c#L47).

For normal dynamic-revision ext2/3/4 primary superblocks, a native label-independent route reads the retained block FD through stdin and formats at most the first 1152 bytes with hexdump -v -n 1152 -e '1/1 "%02x"'. Require exactly 2304 hex characters, magic 53ef at device offset 1080, revision 01000000 at 1100, and nonzero 16-byte UUID at 1128; UUID bytes retain their order. Native ls -dnL /proc/self/fd/N reports the held block node's st_rdev major/minor for comparison with mountinfo. It is metadata inspection, not reopening a pathname for data. Ordinary /proc FD reopening is not a substitute for reading the original inherited block descriptor. Revision-zero, nondefault sb= mounts and other filesystems need separate profiles. [BusyBox ext probe](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/busybox/util-linux/volume_id/ext.c#L35) · [Superblock format](https://docs.kernel.org/filesystems/ext4/super.html) · [Native device listing](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/busybox/coreutils/ls.c#L528).

Read-only installed probes verified synthetic hexdump byte formatting (53ef00ff including NUL), numeric block-FD ls output, and exact-length ext magic/dynamic-revision/nonzero-UUID observations without exposing the UUID. The first manual revision check accidentally addressed offset 1088 (last-check time); it was discarded and repeated using the source-defined 1024+76 offset, which passed. No production code was involved. These probes establish available commands/format only; directory-to-block/mount joins, whole-action supervision, mount-loss behavior and actual write authorization remain unproved.

Pinned opkg accepts explicit local IPKs with nodeps/offline-root/conf-file/tmp-dir; local candidates avoid their own download and nodeps suppresses recursive dependency installation, but flags alone do not constrain all mutations. Upgrades may remove old auto-installed dependencies even without autoremove; force-reinstall first removes matching installed packages and ignores that removal's return before installation. Keep the requested force-reinstall feature, but prove its selected old/new dependency/removal scope and interruption recovery before enabling it. Reject ambiguous conflicts/replacements, unexpected candidates, ownership clashes and unrelated partial state; preserve usable alternate providers. Post-install version/capability checks remain mandatory because same-version/downgrade no-op or failed alternatives handling can still yield success. [Local package selection](https://github.com/openwrt/opkg-lede/blob/80503d94e356476250adaf1f669ee955ec26de76/libopkg/opkg_download.c#L316) · [Orphan cleanup](https://github.com/openwrt/opkg-lede/blob/80503d94e356476250adaf1f669ee955ec26de76/libopkg/opkg_install.c#L334) · [Force reinstall](https://github.com/openwrt/opkg-lede/blob/80503d94e356476250adaf1f669ee955ec26de76/libopkg/opkg_cmd.c#L470). Source-only evidence; command/containment execution remains unproved.

### Mount snapshot parser contract — current package

modules/mountinfo.awk is a pure POSIX awk selector, with no file/command execution. Its caller provides LC_ALL=C, a private stable regular-file snapshot on stdin, its independently checked canonical decimal byte count through cfmgr_mountinfo_size, and a canonical absolute CFMGR_MOUNT_TARGET environment value. Environment transport avoids awk -v escape interpretation for target paths. Bounds: 1–65536 snapshot bytes, 1024 records, 8192 bytes per LF-terminated line including LF, and 4096 target bytes. Raw-control names are outside the supported profile; standard escaped TAB/LF/space/backslash in mount paths are supported. RS28/original-byte-length/exact-EOF framing detects native NUL splitting/truncation.

Validate every record, decimal device/ID text, unique mount IDs, options and standard path escapes before emitting anything. IDs are 1–20 canonical decimal digits, positive for mount IDs and nonnegative for parent/device components; comparison/indexing preserves exact lexical values beyond 2^53. Missing parents outside the snapshot are allowed. Select the deepest component-boundary covering mount and reject duplicate mountpoints at any covering ancestor, including a hidden-child scenario. Compute the target path within the filesystem from the selected bind root plus the relative target. Report pseudo/read-only/noexec mounts as facts; this parser does not approve them for Entware.

Success emits one eleven-field tab-separated record: mount, ID, parent, major:minor, root hex, mountpoint hex, filesystem type, source hex, mount-options hex, super-options hex, filesystem-target hex; then end/body-byte-count and terminal LF. Total output is bounded to 65536 bytes. Hex is byte encoding, not privacy. Consumers must verify status, exact field grammar/count, footer/count/newline and EOF before use; awk write success is insufficient. Exit 0 selects one unambiguous mount; 1 rejects data/ambiguity/bounds; 2 rejects invocation/caller-contract violations; 3 means no covering mount. Failures emit no partial stdout or raw diagnostics. Acquisition, UUID/ownership/writability, current namespace/retained-FD checks, supervision and mount-loss protection remain separate gates.

**Topology extension:** explicit `cfmgr_mountinfo_mode=topology` retains that mount record and appends a second record containing `topology`, selected shared/master/propagate-from IDs (or `-`), unbindable 0/1, unknown selected optional-tag count, and strict descendant-mount record count. The terminal footer covers both records. Missing/empty mode or `select` preserves the original output; other modes fail with status 2. Count every validated strict component-boundary descendant record, including hidden/duplicate descendants, independently of input order. Equal targets and unrelated paths are excluded. Selected propagation IDs remain exact decimal text, and unrelated optional fields do not affect selected facts. These observations support a later private staging-root policy; they are not approval to mount, execute or write. The existing IO/storage consumers keep the original mode unchanged.

**Complete startup closure audit, 2026-10-08:** fifteen official library IPKs (4,285,201 archive bytes) matched saved official-index sizes and SHA256 and were inspected only as inert bytes. For timeout 9.9-2, all three maintained feeds need `ld-2.27.so`, `libc-2.27.so`, `libpthread-2.27.so` and `librt-2.27.so`; ARM/MIPS additionally need `libgcc_s.so.1`. Gzip 1.14-1 needs the same loader/libc, plus libgcc on ARM/MIPS. The loader aliases are `ld-linux-aarch64.so.1`, `ld-linux.so.3` and `ld.so.1` respectively, each a relative sibling link to `ld-2.27.so`. Other required sibling links are `libc.so.6`, `libpthread.so.0` and `librt.so.1` to their corresponding regular files. Every path is beneath `opt/lib`; libgcc is already a regular file. These graphs have no unresolved needed-library edge, link cycle, absolute link, DSO RPATH/RUNPATH or audit/filter additions. The tools alone have RUNPATH `/opt/lib`. `libssp` and the `coreutils` metapackage are package dependencies, not additional startup ELF dependencies for these two programs. A DSO's own `/lib/...` interpreter is irrelevant when that object is loaded as a library rather than executed.

All three audited loader binaries contain `/opt/etc/ld.so.preload`. Glibc skips a truly empty preload file, but an absent/empty observation on mutable retained Opt is not permanent admission; runtime flags do not suppress a later nonempty preload. The next root design must preserve admitted file contents and loader lookup paths through execution. No base-library repair/upgrade is authorized by this audit, and it proves neither symbol compatibility nor actual Entware execution. Provenance, archive/member hashes and exact ELF/link records are retained in ignored `.tmp/contained-tools/audit.md`, `library-audit.json` and `closure-audit.json`. **Startup supervision decision:** a native wrapper's complete terminal record may establish that its synchronous chroot command returned without an unbounded owner `wait`. Publish outside the chroot, opening the terminal file only after the command returns; no terminal descriptor may reach the contained program. Clear inherited cleanup traps, close external descriptors, use native cwd `/`, and make terminal publication the last guard operation before explicit exit. Owner polling expiry or partial/malformed completion retains the active guard; it must not be mapped to a fake signal or treated as ordinary cleanup permission. Valid completion still requires the exact mapped-user unmount barrier before deleting staging. Coreutils `--foreground` avoids unchecked process-group setup and is limited here to fixed nonforking probes. Its source blocks cleanup signals around reaping its direct child, but asynchronous native ash can leave INT/QUIT ignored. No owner cancellation forwarding, arbitrary descendant supervision, wrapper reaping or hard kernel deadline is claimed. The first helper package remains unconnected to the synchronous lifecycle until its explicit completion gate and immutable admission are implemented. Parent and focused read-only architecture review agree on this boundary; execution tests remain pending.

**Native supervision helper — focused validation complete:** `modules/supervision.sh` defines only the fixed timeout-version and timeout-wrapped gzip-version probes plus explicit fixture entry. A fresh mode-700 `guard/probe` outside the execution root holds exclusive captures and a terminal record. Production chroot is fixed at `/usr/sbin/chroot`; child environment is cleared, cwd is `/`, known external FD3–9 are closed and inherited cleanup traps are reset. Caller tracing must already be off and no other external descriptors may be inherited. The native wrapper opens its terminal output only after synchronous env/chroot returns, writes its complete bounded record as the final guard operation and explicitly exits. Readiness polling allows five native one-second sleeps for timeout or six for the three-second gzip work budget plus one-second KILL escalation and startup allowance. No numeric PID cancellation, unbounded wait, mount change or resource removal is performed by this helper.

Started/completed state is separate from child status: completed child 124 is distinguishable from polling expiry 124 with completion unproved. Malformed/torn records publish no child status. A valid terminal record still proves completion when captures are oversized, but the helper returns failure. Accepted stdout/stderr are each at most 4096 bytes; nine file-limit units bound physical output to 4608 or 9216 bytes per stream on the checked 512/1024-unit shells. This is one attempt per guard, with strict canonical paths, fixed commands, fresh state per call and no reuse/adoption of an existing probe directory. Caller-owned cleanup and admission remain separate; this code does not make the old synchronous callback safe for background work.

Coverage includes fixed arguments/clean environment, real FD3–9 closure with caller preservation, traps/options/IFS/umask, exact framing/NUL/EOF/status, quiet admission, interrupted/failed startup, late completion and physical/accepted capture limits. **Execution-image amendment — implemented for internal fixed probes; operational artifact admission pending:** separate executable bytes from the mutable installation destination. Build a small private RAM tree containing only admitted executable/library bytes and fixed sibling links; bind that tree at the execution root's `/opt`. Put timeout/gzip and any later approved static opkg under that bind's `libexec`, with fixed `/bootstrap` symlinks if needed. Keep its `/opt/etc/ld.so.preload`, loader cache, extra modules, shell and proc absent. Close all outside directory descriptors before chroot and use a clean C-locale environment. The actual retained Entware volume, when needed for later installation, is a separate bind at `/offline/opt`; probes need no writable installation target. Standard interpreter/RUNPATH resolution then reaches the fixed execution image, and executable mappings directly pin its owned bind. Every admitted symlink/interpreter/dependency must resolve within that image; hashes alone do not prove the graph. This supersedes using mutable installed libraries/preload directly for contained execution, while preserving the existing synchronous native callback profile.

Copy only bounded regular-file inputs using native tools and verify the final private copies against approved artifact/member identity before execution. Reusing verified installed base-library bytes is allowed; missing/broken/unknown base libraries still fail closed, with no automatic base-library repair. Provenance/index/archive/member validation remains a separate gate, not supplied by a caller's arbitrary hash. The measured union of timeout/gzip plus libraries is six regular files/four links and 1,838,584 bytes on AArch64, seven/four and 1,667,228 on ARM, seven/four and 2,212,904 on MIPS. Proposed initial limits are four MiB per regular file and eight MiB aggregate, plus fixed metadata, enforced before copying; these are resource limits rather than support promises for other versions.

The image bind must be checked read-only, nosuid/nodev and executable before launch; directory mode alone does not restrain root. Its outside RAM alias remains under the trusted owner's no-more-writes contract after final verification. Source-supported commands for validated ordinary absolute source/target paths are `mount -n -i -o bind,ro SOURCE TARGET`, `mount -n -i -o make-private TARGET`, and `mount -n -i -o remount,bind,ro,nosuid,nodev,exec SOURCE TARGET`. Admit a non-shared destination parent before binding; later private conversion cannot undo propagated peers. Initial bind ignores `ro` at the kernel boundary, but including it avoids BusyBox's automatic read-only retry. The explicit two-operand remount bypasses mount-table reads and changes only the new mount; omitted nosuid/nodev/noexec flags would otherwise be cleared. Verify the same exact mount ID/identity and final flags/private/no-descendant state after every step. No fallback mutation or superblock remount. Existing cleanup's prohibition on read-only remount as a substitute for removal is unchanged.

With native-launch completion established, remove the exact execution-image bind first using ordinary unmount, then any retained installation bind and null bind, proving each absent before RAM cleanup. Unproved launch, busy/changed mounts or any failed mutation retains the guard. The existing Linux static/dynamic mapping proof and pinned kernel exec ordering support this design; the topology/read-only sequence and assembled lifecycle now have passing controlled Linux kernel proofs at the current checkpoint. Restricted opkg configuration/process/write admission remains separate. Evidence: `.tmp/contained-tools/readonly-bind.md`, its provenance file, and the closure audit above. [Native mount implementation](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/busybox/util-linux/mount.c#L2192-L2259) · [Per-mount remount flags](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src-rt-5.02axhnd/kernel/linux-4.1/fs/namespace.c#L2203-L2292).

### Private IO and mount acquisition — current package

modules/io.sh defines isolated internal workspace/capture/snapshot operations; sourcing it performs no work. The caller supplies a trusted RAM parent and controlled callback/verified parser, with tracing disabled before passing arguments. Native firmware tools are trusted and selected from fixed paths; no inherited Opt/tool override. The explicit fixture API is not exposed by the CLI. An owned mode-700 workspace uses at most eight mkdir attempts; cleanup touches only that successfully created workspace and must complete before snapshot publication.

Captures consume slots 0–15, including failed attempts, with exclusive mode-600 out/err/status files and no reuse/overwrite. Each requested stream cap is 0–65536 bytes. The file-size limit permits at least one overflow byte; post-capture byte checks enforce exact caps. Across 512/1024-byte shell limit units, the physical ceiling is 132096 bytes per stream and 4227072 bytes across all streams, plus small status files; accepted stream payload is at most 2097152 bytes. A valid status ledger records producer exit and both exact counts. Capture return zero means the ledger is complete, never producer success by itself; consumers must check both. IO/limit failures return 1, usage 2, and signals retain 129/130/143.

The mount helper reads fixed /proc/self/mountinfo into a private stable capture, passes its exact nonzero byte count and C locale to mountinfo.awk, then verifies parser status, eleven nonempty fields, numeric/hex/path/filesystem grammar, exact two-line framing/footer/EOF and byte count before buffering at most 64 KiB for post-cleanup publication. Parser status 3 retains no-covering-mount meaning. Consumers must still check final command status and complete framing because a failed pipe write may be partial. This is snapshot evidence, not persistent UUID, writable-volume or hotplug-safe authorization.

**Topology acquisition package:** `cfmgr_io_topology_snapshot ROOT TARGET PARSER` uses the same fixed proc source, two capture slots, native tool selection and report ownership. Its explicit fixture action alone accepts a synthetic input. Verify the ordinary mount record plus seven topology fields, positive decimal IDs or `-`, unbindable 0/1, canonical unknown count at most 4096 and descendant count at most 1023. Validate counts before arithmetic and verify all three LF-terminated records, combined body-byte footer and exact original byte count. The internal capture's optional sixth literal mode preserves five-argument callers and clears inherited/stale topology state. Unknown/shared facts remain observations; no storage policy or mount operation is introduced. Focused validation passed 227 tests / 1 missing-BusyBox skip, with 73 unrelated storage cases deselected, in 498.80 seconds. Ruff lint/format, compilation, ShellCheck, POSIX shfmt, sh syntax, whitespace and parent review passed. No production policy, mount operation or CLI action was added.

**Supervision source boundary:** coreutils 9.9 timeout creates its process group before forking but does not check `setpgid` success. It waits only for its direct child; that child's exit can cancel later KILL escalation while grandchildren remain. Neither timeout exit nor status 124/137 alone permits mount/workspace cleanup. A saved PID/start-time check followed by a numeric kill is not an atomic process lease. The next proof must constrain the admitted process tree, establish group ownership and terminal descendants, or retain an unresolved guard. A cooperative in-group controller is a candidate, but would require an audited shell/helper closure inside the isolated root; no extra runtime dependency has been selected. The restricted opkg/gzip route is being assessed before expanding that closure. Prebootstrap blocked syscalls and uninterruptible storage IO remain outside a hard reaping guarantee. [Official timeout source](https://github.com/coreutils/coreutils/blob/v9.9/src/timeout.c#L533-L600). Evidence: ignored `.tmp/review-supervision-timeout-9.9.c`.

The narrower candidate uses a finite timeout for each admitted phase and stops admitting phases after cancellation or mount loss. Its remaining deadline may provide bounded cancellation latency without an outer saved-PID kill; exact budgets and signal handling are not frozen. Opkg's known extraction path starts gzip children and a copy thread. `gzip_close` attempts to kill/wait for its child, but ignores a failed wait, so return status alone is insufficient. Prove the complete restricted process graph and the terminal state of the whole opkg process, including threads, before relying on a bounded group-membership scan. A zombie leader alone is not that proof. Unexpected members, incomplete reads or unresolved IO retain the guard and workspace. No controller/shell dependency is selected merely to improve uncertain cleanup.

The complete pinned opkg source scan supports gzip-only external execution under a strict local-IPK profile: empty feed configuration/lists, preserved explicit local candidates, private config/offline root/temp, `--nodeps`, no signature subprocess, no scripts/conffiles or unrelated incomplete state, safe dependency/provider/alternative ownership, and empty interceptor state with no helper shadow. The optional force-reinstall path still ignores its removal result and internally forces dependency removal, so preflight/postconditions remain required. SIGINT can exit after writing package status without waiting for gzip. The exact-bind cleanup proof above supersedes a general process-group scan as the next implementation direction for this restricted root; it does not make opkg exit or a saved PID authoritative. Evidence: ignored `.tmp/opkg-process-graph/audit.md` and pinned source captures.

**Older-kernel candidate:** a later bind-based profile may establish the new bind's filesystem/device/root identity even when the original descriptor lacks `mnt_id`. The matched kernel binds the held descriptor's actual path and reports the clone's filesystem-relative root and actual mount flags; UUID still requires independent block-device evidence. This does not recover original mount-instance continuity, and an already-detached source may reject binding. Older kernel/native-command evidence and lifecycle tests remain required. Keep the current read-only observer's `mnt_id` requirement unchanged; this source finding does not add supported firmware. [Matched bind source](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src-rt-5.02axhnd/kernel/linux-4.1/fs/namespace.c#L2149-L2181).

Workspace callbacks are bounded trusted internal code and have no output publication channel. The isolated owner preserves external caller state and handles signals delivered to itself; an external caller must route signals/supervise. Hung native commands, SIGKILL, uninterruptible storage IO and background-writing callbacks are not made safe by a shell trap. Supervision and retained-volume approval remain separate packages. Focused validation passed 147 host cases with 1 explicit missing-BusyBox skip; full integration passed 927/7. Final whitespace-only IFS preservation passed nine targeted regressions plus static/whitespace checks. Parent and independent delta review accepted the narrow contract. Published a78d007 passed Linux run 37735600527: 934 tests, including seven dedicated BusyBox cases, no skips, plus all static checks.

### Native storage observation parser — validated foundation

modules/storageinfo.awk accepts fdinfo or blkid mode, LC_ALL=C, no operands, an independently checked canonical size of 0–4096, and a caller-owned stable regular input. The original-byte/RS28/exact-EOF checks prevent raw NUL/control splitting from producing accepted partial observations. Both modes validate everything before emitting a bounded ASCII body plus end/body-byte-count and final LF. Consumers still verify producer status, framing and exact EOF.

Fdinfo permits at most 64 LF-terminated records with unique ASCII keys. Require canonical nonnegative pos (at most 20 digits), octal flags (at most 16 digits), and positive canonical mnt_id (at most 20 digits) for success. Preserve mount IDs as text beyond 2^53; tolerate bounded printable unknown fields without interpreting them. Valid legacy records missing mnt_id return 3. Empty/malformed records return 1.

Blkid accepts one LF-terminated native record whose device prefix exactly matches the canonical /dev path supplied through CFMGR_BLKID_DEVICE, avoiding awk -v escape interpretation. Only unique quoted LABEL, UUID and TYPE attributes in native order are supported. UUID is 1–128 ASCII hex/hyphen bytes; TYPE is 1–64 ASCII filesystem-name bytes. Empty output, missing UUID, or any well-formed LABEL-bearing observation returns 3 without identity. Explicit empty UUID and malformed/duplicate/out-of-order/quote-ambiguous records return 1. Label-free success emits byte-encoded UUID and optional TYPE; it is an observation, not authority to use a volume. Invocation errors return 2; all failures are quiet. Labelled ext volumes use the independent held-descriptor observation route below; blkid labels remain insufficient identity evidence.

Focused tests passed 128 host cases with one explicit missing-BusyBox skip; independent source review found no blockers. Full integration passed 1055 tests with eight explicit missing-BusyBox skips in 366.67 seconds, with all static checks. Published d6240dc passed Linux run 37737349140: 1063 tests in 113.18 seconds, no skips, including eight dedicated BusyBox cases and all static checks. The package performs no source reads, device access, retained-FD acquisition, filesystem writes or installed-code execution.

### Retained-volume observation — focused validation complete

This package adds modules/storage.sh without operational CLI integration. The production observer uses fixed /opt and proc inputs inside the private IO owner. Guarded function-call redirections hold directory FD9 and block FD8 across acquisition, comparisons and result staging; normal returns restore them before cleanup/publication. Native command/exec wrappers are not available on the measured firmware. Retaining a descriptor during this observation does not transfer a storage lease to its caller. The opened FD9 itself must pass the directory-type check; a pre-open path check alone leaves a replacement race.

Compare the canonical current target, selected mount record, inherited directory mnt_id and native test -ef before and after the primary-superblock read. Require ext2/ext3/ext4, reject sb= options before block-data acquisition, validate the canonical /dev source and require block type plus matching numeric rdev from the original FD8. Read exactly the supported dynamic-revision primary-superblock prefix through FD8 stdin, never by reopening its displayed proc path. Extend storageinfo.awk with strict blockdev and exthex modes, preserving original-byte bounds and terminal framing. Unsupported or incomplete identities remain unavailable; labels cannot affect the independent binary UUID read.

Use at most 16 capture slots. Factor reusable checked mount acquisition and add a separate report owner with one bounded ASCII staging operation; status-only callbacks keep their existing contract. The volume ledger contains mount ID, major/minor, filesystem type, byte-encoded root/mountpoint/filesystem-relative target/options and UUID, followed by the terminal byte count. Publish only after all producer statuses, empty stderr, exact framing and owned cleanup succeed. Matching before/after observations do not prove atomic continuity, device-generation stability, actual writability, filesystem health or expected configured identity. Blocked IO, command supervision and native performance remain separate gates; slot count is not a process-count or CPU-cost claim. Host fixtures and unprivileged Linux descriptor tests supply this stage's evidence; no additional router execution is needed.

- [x] Separate report ownership, reusable checked mount captures and fixed native producer allowlist; existing workspace behavior preserved.
- [x] Retained-directory type/mount-ID joins, original block-FD data read, before/after comparisons and exact framed volume output.
- [x] Strict blockdev/exthex parsers, including lexical device numbers and original UUID byte order.
- [x] Focused validation: 395 passed / 4 skipped in 289.26 seconds; skips are three unavailable-BusyBox cases and one Linux-only proc descriptor proof. Ruff, ShellCheck, POSIX shfmt and shell syntax passed. Parent and independent final review accepted the frozen package.
- [x] Full local tools/check.py: 1176 passed / 10 explicit skips in 416.89 seconds, all static checks passed.
- [x] Major checkpoint published: c841900 passed Linux CI 37743072398 with 1186 passes / no skips in 147.93 seconds, including all nine dedicated BusyBox cases and the Linux descriptor proof. All static checks passed; parent verified the exact head and remote refs.

Linux native-test fixtures explicitly preserve FD9 into the real test command. This is host/source evidence; assembled router performance, physical hotplug, supervision and mutation authority remain pending. No parent product-code takeover occurred.

This lets later isolated-root setup bind the observed directory before those descriptors close; it is not permission to write through a reopened public path. The status-only owner discards callback output and preserves failure/signal/cleanup handling. Existing observe output and acquisition order remain unchanged. Callbacks own their external guard and cleanup, never mount beneath IO scratch, and leave no asynchronous users of it. This API does not create mounts or execute Entware code.

### Modular distribution and source selection — selected during implementation

Repository layout is cfmgr.sh plus directly used shell/awk files in modules/; there is no compilation into a main script. The installed command remains cfmgr. At final documentation polish, after the real installer and acceptance path exist, provide one copy-and-paste POSIX command that downloads the installer, applies its selected executable permissions and invokes it through success-only && chaining. A readable multiline form may use backslash-newline with no trailing spaces. Validate the exact command and its failure behavior against the selected installer; do not add a premature install command to the current development README.

Latest user selection supersedes the separate remote-catalog proposal: ship catalog defaults with CFMgr and store the active catalog in `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/config`, with this exact case. The user may manually edit its `branch:` selector and named module URLs for forks/testing; no menu setter or `catalog_url` indirection. Default is `main`; accept `develop` or a full commit hash. The manual `developer` flag defaults to false. With developer=false, updates/reinstalls may replace only the catalog portion with the selected package defaults. With developer=true, preserve any existing catalog even during explicitly confirmed force reinstall; initialize defaults only if absent. Malformed existing catalog requires repair, never destructive defaulting. Existing manager-check suppression remains in force; future developer behaviors share this flag. Preserve credentials, feature/guard state and unrelated config fields. Current actions retain their pinned selection even if the committed catalog changes. The [development guide](docs/development.md) documents usage; exact enclosing config schema remains P1.3 work.

Use readable separate source modules. Named catalog URL entries use a literal `{commit}` placeholder. Resolve a selected branch once to a full immutable commit before downloads. A commit identifies the entire tree, including unchanged files at their latest version as of that commit. Fetch the trusted manifest and modules from that snapshot; never scan later history, follow per-file moving branches, or fill absent historical modules from current main/develop. Parse catalog values as data, never source/eval. Require validated HTTPS URL forms in the same explicit repository and safe owned destinations. Invalid source overrides fail the affected action without silent default fallback. Selection does not waive downgrade/version/schema/hash rules or enable developer mode.

Installation/reinstallation/repair stages and verifies the complete required package before activation. Normal startup/status/hooks use installed code and defer when a required helper is missing. Each invocation pins one compatible generation. Verify trusted manifest/version and every required file hash before source/execute, atomically activate a complete package, and retain the prior usable updater/recovery path. Exact grammar/resource limits, generation layout/reader retention, bootstrap trust and interruption/power-loss behavior remain P1.5/P1.7/P1.16 gates before downloader implementation. No self-unpacking monolith or runtime Python/archive dependency is selected. Shipped catalogs/manifests remain with local code pending publication approval, regardless of text extension.

Official Skynet v9.0.5 stages/hash-compares individual files with trap rollback; vpnmgr v3.3.1 downloads separate provider modules. Neither inspected path proves one immutable externally hash-bound complete generation, so their installer guarantees are not inherited. [Skynet downloader](https://github.com/Adamm00/IPSet_ASUS/blob/166850143a4d786e522f15f6817131b9e6f07046/firewall.sh#L8433) · [Skynet updater](https://github.com/Adamm00/IPSet_ASUS/blob/166850143a4d786e522f15f6817131b9e6f07046/firewall.sh#L12949) · [vpnmgr modules](https://github.com/h0me5k1n/vpnmgr/blob/35c7b629e129d9ce4018764d9e06120738e6fe0c/vpnmgr.sh#L668).

### Native primitive contract — first implementation package

The first `modules/common.sh` package defines pure POSIX shell helpers only. Sourcing defines functions without commands, IO, process launches, or changes to caller state. It does not provide an installer, updater, provider client, config reader, or readiness dispatcher.

| Helper | Frozen input/output contract |
| --- | --- |
| `cfmgr_decimal_normalize` | One ASCII decimal string of 1–128 digits; remove leading zeros and emit canonical decimal text, with all-zero input becoming `0`. No whole-value arithmetic conversion. |
| `cfmgr_decimal_compare` | Two such decimal strings; compare normalized length and individual digits, emitting `-1`, `0`, or `1`. Loop/count arithmetic stays small regardless of input magnitude. |
| `cfmgr_uint31` | Normalize and accept only `0` through `2147483647`, using text-safe range checking before any future integer use. |
| `cfmgr_version_compare` | Two supported numeric versions, each at most 128 bytes and exactly three dot-separated canonical decimal components. No prefix, suffix, prerelease or leading component zeros. Compare components without host-width assumptions. This is a restricted release format, not a general SemVer parser. |
| `cfmgr_sha256_normalize` | Exactly 64 ASCII hexadecimal characters; emit lower-case digest text. Reject labels, whitespace, additional digests and invalid characters. Backend invocation, file ownership and hash verification are separate later work. |

Wrong argument counts or malformed/out-of-bounds input fail without successful output or raw-input diagnostics. Helpers isolate scratch state, require no external tools and never evaluate data. Tests exercise both host `/bin/sh` and available `dash`, use deterministic integer/version oracles and hostile inputs, and distinguish these parser checks from complete V32 package validation or actual BusyBox/router acceptance.

### Native JSON parser proof contract — current package

The next independent source module is `modules/json.awk`, tested from the developer venv. It is a parser proof, not yet a provider/config/transport integration. Pinned BusyBox 1.25.1 and older 1.20.2 awk treat raw NUL as an alternate record separator and store C strings; blindly rebuilding newline-separated records can accept truncated/reconstructed data. [Current awk](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/busybox/editors/awk.c#L2236) · [older awk](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin/blob/4a09301907eca9e55db992c5c0002a8688388255/release/src/router/busybox/editors/awk.c#L1886).

Caller precondition: an owned stable bounded regular file, independently checked successful native `wc -c`, and LC_ALL=C. The parser sets a raw control separator illegal in JSON, reads exactly one record, requires its byte length to equal the independent count, and requires the next read to return EOF rather than another record/read error. No line reconstruction, unchecked pipe producer, or source/eval. Native wc counts NUL and reports read errors in the matched source. Owned staging, producer status, stream deadlines and exact-file identity remain later P1 transport/IO gates; this parser does not make arbitrary stdin safe.

Initial parser caps: 65536 input bytes, 4096 value nodes, 32 nested containers, 16384 decoded UTF-8 bytes per string, and 131072 token-output bytes. Validate UTF-8 scalar sequences and JSON grammar/escapes/surrogates; reject decoded duplicate keys without Unicode normalization. Preserve JSON numbers as lexical text, never shell/awk numeric values. Store decoded strings/keys as ASCII UTF-8 hex, including escaped U+0000, so no NUL enters a C-string/shell value. A later typed consumer must enforce its own allowable field contents.

Require explicit `cfmgr_json_size` and mode `validate` or `tokens`, with no filename operands. The caller supplies an already validated canonical decimal count and a literal mode, because awk -v assignment parsing interprets escapes before the program runs. Validation emits nothing. Token mode emits bounded preorder tab-separated id/parent/location/type/payload only after full validation: root location r; object keys k+UTF8hex; array index i+decimal; string payload x+UTF8hex; numeric payload n+original lexeme; containers/true/false/null use -. Append a terminal end/node-count/body-byte-count record; the 131072-byte cap includes that footer before output begins. Future consumers must verify exact footer-at-EOF/newline, body bytes, record count/sequential IDs and parser status before using tokens. This catches incomplete output when native awk does not report write errors, but does not replace owned-file integrity/durability checks. Validation errors and exhausted limits emit no partial successful stdout or secret-bearing diagnostics. Host independent JSON/lexeme oracles, malformed byte/Unicode/duplicate/cap fixtures and optional real BusyBox checks precede integration. These remain partial V42 parser evidence, not a completed native provider profile.

Matched awk ignores fwrite/fputs/fflush return values and exits using its stored program status; footer framing is therefore required before a consumer trusts token output. [Output implementation](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/busybox/editors/awk.c#L2983) · [exit implementation](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/busybox/editors/awk.c#L3558).

Read-only installed-tool probes confirmed the input assumptions: A/NUL/B/newline has wc byte count 4 but two awk records of lengths 1 and 2; the selected raw separator preserves a nine-byte multiline JSON record and then EOF; byte mappings 1–255 each retain one byte under LC_ALL=C; the large numeric substring 9007199254740993 remains unchanged. These are synthetic command-capability probes only, not execution of the new parser. The SSH session was closed after reads.

### Selected-WAN and family evidence — source review

Matched firmware source confirms that native DDNS normally selects the primary WAN, with explicit DDNS-unit/fallback handling in load-balance mode. PPP IPv4 and IPv6 can use different logical/physical interfaces. On the sampled GT-AX11000 target configuration, the IPv6 unit-zero branch is indicated separately from the IPv4 primary; installed behavior still needs corroboration. [DDNS selection](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/rc/services.c#L4604) · [interfaces](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/shared/rtstate.c#L359) · [IPv6 unit selection](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/shared/rtstate.c#L590).

Explicit known administrative disable can establish inactivity within the selected unit/family. Empty or unrecognized protocol/service values remain unknown even where firmware coercion calls them disabled. DHCP/DHCP6 deconfiguration clears learned addresses during configured enabled service; an empty address/default route or disconnection cannot authorize provider cleanup. A valid observed address still requires current family-specific egress/public classification; configured NVRAM values alone are not current public state. [IPv6 mode coercion](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/shared/misc.c#L1924) · [DHCP6 deconfiguration](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/rc/udhcpc.c#L1356).

Neither ntp_ready nor link_internet is fresh Internet proof: the latter can be set to connected after failed detection when no-Internet Web redirection is disabled. Native firmware's IPv6 reader also ignores tentative/DAD-failed/deprecated flags when selecting a scope-zero address; CFMgr cannot copy that as publish validation. Remaining P1.13/P1.17 probes cover whitelisted state selection, native ip options, masked kernel address/route/rule evidence and explicit family egress readiness. No general enabled-uplink IPv4 absence proof or complete IPv6-only support has been established. [Connectivity coercion](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/rc/wanduck.c#L1243) · [IPv6 reader](https://github.com/RMerl/asuswrt-merlin.ng/blob/433fc608f5fc1689f3d3c3ab5cd2a7d57102260f/release/src/router/rc/services.c#L4449).

### Address normalization contract — current package

modules/ip.sh defines cfmgr_ipv4_normalize and cfmgr_ipv6_normalize with exactly one host-address argument, one canonical line on success, and nonzero/empty output on invalid input. IPv4 requires four ASCII decimal octets 0–255 with no leading zero except literal 0, maximum 15 characters. IPv6 accepts RFC 4291 host forms including one legal double-colon and a strict terminal dotted-IPv4 tail, maximum 45 ASCII characters. Reject whitespace/controls, zones, CIDR and bracket/socket syntax. Valid colon/hextet text such as ::1:80 remains an IPv6 host, not an inferred port.

IPv6 output uses lowercase shortest hextets and the longest zero run of at least two groups, with leftmost tie-breaking. Dotted IPv4 tails become hexadecimal groups, including mapped addresses. Python 3.14 changed its mapped compressed display to dotted notation; tests correct only that oracle presentation using fixed mapped vectors, numeric address equality and idempotence. Other addresses retain the independent Python compressed oracle. Syntax acceptance includes private, reserved, link-local and unspecified addresses; no public/publishing eligibility is implied. That classification remains P1.13/P1.17 work.

Math is bounded to octets/hextets (maximum 65535), with no whole-address integer conversion or eval. Subshell function bodies isolate caller IFS/options/positionals/scratch. Sourcing defines functions without actions. Host sh/dash checks pass with no extra parser executable. On the actual target, read-only type probes confirmed printf/test/[ resolve to /usr/bin firmware applets, while local/read are builtins; therefore no claim of zero external native processes or PATH-empty firmware support is made. Full native source execution remains untested; native hexadecimal printf capability alone was probed.
